File: .agents/skills/bundle/SKILL.md
================
---
name: bundle
description: >-
  Bundle the codebase into clean AI-friendly XML chunks with a strict maximum limit of 130,000 characters per file, grouped by directory boundaries, followed by an automated character audit.
  Triggered by #bundle, /bundle, #repomix-split, /repomix-split, or requests to package the codebase into chunks.
---

# Codebase Bundling & 130k-Chunking Workflow (`#bundle`)

**Purpose:** Package the repository into clean, well-formed XML chunks using Repomix, ensuring no individual file chunk exceeds 130,000 characters. Files are grouped atomically by directory structure so that code is never sliced midway.

---

## 1. Execution Modes

### A. Default Bundling (`repomix-output/`)
Pack the codebase into the default output directory `repomix-output/`:

**PowerShell:**
```powershell
.\repomix
# or:
.\repomix.ps1
```

**CMD / Batch:**
```cmd
repomix.bat
```

**NPM:**
```powershell
npm run repomix
```

### B. Custom Destination Folder (`repomix_<name>`)
Specify a custom folder name as the first argument:

**PowerShell:**
```powershell
.\repomix v1_release
# or:
.\repomix.ps1 v1_release
```

**CMD / Batch:**
```cmd
repomix.bat v1_release
```
*Note:* The runner automatically normalizes the folder name with the `repomix_` prefix (e.g. `repomix_v1_release`), ensuring it is automatically ignored by `.gitignore` and `.repomixignore`.

### C. Additional Repomix Flags
Pass any additional Repomix CLI flags after the folder name (or as the first argument if starting with `--`):

**PowerShell:**
```powershell
.\repomix my_chunk --no-security-check
.\repomix --no-security-check
```

**CMD / Batch:**
```cmd
repomix.bat my_chunk --no-security-check
repomix.bat --no-security-check
```

---

## 2. Chunking Specifications & Architecture

- **Max Character Ceiling:** 130,000 characters per file.
- **Chunk Size Budget:** 120,000 bytes (`output.splitOutput: 120000` in `repomix.config.json`).
- **Atomic Integrity:** Top-level directory grouping ensures functions, classes, and components are never cut midway.
- **Self-Contained Chunks:** Each chunk includes a `<file_summary>`, directory structure tree, and properly closed XML tags (`</file>`, `</files>`).
- **Automated Verification:** The runner immediately executes `scripts/patches_and_tests/repomix-audit.ps1` to inspect every generated file and report character counts and `[OK] <= 130k` status.

================
File: .agents/skills/deep-refactor/SKILL.md
================
---
name: deep-refactor
description: >-
  Decompose large components, hooks, or server files (>300-500 lines) into modular subroutines, hooks, and utilities with 100% behavior parity. Supports step-by-step Batch Mode and fast One-Shot Blueprint Mode.
  Triggered by #deep-refactor, /deep-refactor, #refrag, /refrag, #oneshot, or /oneshot.
---

# Deep-Refactor Workflow (`#deep-refactor`)

**Purpose:** Safely decompose monolithic files into focused hooks (`src/hooks/`), pure utilities (`src/utils/` or `*Utils.ts`), types (`*.types.ts`), and leaf components without changing observable runtime behavior.

---

## 1. Execution Modes (Choose Based on Context)

### Mode A: Interactive Batch Mode _(Default when exploring & refactoring live)_

- **Limit:** Maximum **3 implementation files** per batch (test files `*.test.ts` and docs `*.md` do not count toward the limit).
- **Validation per Batch:** Run `npm run lint` and `npm test` after each batch and create a checkpoint commit on `preview`.
- **Handoff Checkpoint:** Pause after Batch 3 to offer a concise session-handoff summary unless Auto-Pilot is active.
- **Recommended Model:** `PRO` or `Flash (High Thinking)`.

### Mode B: One-Shot Blueprint Mode _(Triggered by `#oneshot`, "alle auf einmal", or when a complete plan/code blueprint is provided)_

- **No Batch/File Limit:** Write all new and updated modules in a **single pass** without intermediate stops.
- **Single Validation Pass:** Run `npx prettier --write <scoped-files>`, `npm run lint`, `npm test`, and `npm run check` **once** after all files are written.
- **Recommended Model:** `Flash (Low or Medium Thinking)` for maximum speed when applying pre-verified code. When using Flash, strictly follow the Single-Action Protocol from `flash/SKILL.md`.

---

## 2. Non-Negotiable Guardrails (Both Modes)

1. **100% Behavior & Export Parity:**
   - Preserve all public props, named/default exports, and re-exported types so parent callers require zero changes unless explicitly in scope.
   - Preserve all DOM querySelector classes (e.g., `.zoom-media-container`, `.media-zoom-target`, `[data-video-controls]`), inline math (letterbox/pillarbox geometry), event capture order (`onPointerDownCapture` vs `onClickCapture`), and platform keys (`ios-stable-video`).
   - Keep `useEffect`, `useMemo`, and `useCallback` dependency arrays semantically identical.
2. **Dirty Working Tree Protection:**
   - Run `git status --short` in Preflight (B0).
   - Never stage, revert, format, or overwrite pre-existing modified or untracked user files.
   - Never use `git add .`, `git reset`, `git checkout`, or `git clean`. Always stage explicit file paths.
3. **Branch & Remote Safety:**
   - Commit only on `preview` or a dedicated feature branch (`never` on `main`/`master`).
   - Never run `git push` automatically.

---

## 3. Standard Execution Phases

1. **Phase 0 (Preflight):** Check `git status --short`, inspect target file + all callers, record baseline for `npm run lint` and `npm test`.
2. **Phase 1 (Pure Utils & Types):** Extract `*.types.ts` and pure helper functions (`*Utils.ts`) + optional unit test (`*Utils.test.ts`).
3. **Phase 2 (Custom Hooks):** Extract state, timers, DOM observers, and event capture handlers into `src/hooks/<domain>/` and update `index.ts` barrel exports.
4. **Phase 3 (Leaf Components & Orchestrator):** Extract isolated JSX sub-trees into leaf components and reduce the main component to a clean orchestrator (<200 lines).
5. **Phase 4 (Documentation Sync — Mandatory):** Check whether `AGENTS.md` or any file in `.agents/rules/` (`core-react-state.md`, `mobile-touch.md`, `video-audio-media.md`, `styling-ui.md`, `cloudflare-architecture.md`) references the refactored file and update the architecture notes accordingly.
6. **Phase 5 (Final Verification & Commit):** Run scoped Prettier, `npm run lint`, `npm test`, `npm run check`, bump `package.json` patch version once (if `package.json` is clean), commit on `preview`, and output a concise Completion Report (line-count delta + parity checklist).

================
File: .agents/skills/delegate/SKILL.md
================
---
name: delegate
description: >-
  Writes a focused execution plan to an Artifact and spawns a subagent to execute it from the file, preventing context bloat.
  Triggered by #delegate, /delegate, #deligate, or /deligate.
---

# Delegate Workflow

- **Purpose:** To prevent conversation bloat by giving a subagent a pristine, noise-free instruction set. This is the programmatic equivalent of starting a brand new chat just for execution.
- **Step 1: Create the Context Artifact:**
  - Write the exact step-by-step implementation instructions, file paths, and required context into a Markdown Artifact using the `write_to_file` tool.
  - You MUST provide `ArtifactMetadata` when creating this file.
  - You MUST set `RequestFeedback: false` so that the workflow does not pause. The artifact is strictly for the user's visual reference.
- **Step 2: Spawn the Subagent:**
  - Immediately after creating the artifact, use the `invoke_subagent` tool.
  - **Model Selection (3-Tier Intelligent Routing):**
    1. **Explicit Override:** If the prompt explicitly contains the word `flash` (with or without `#`), invoke the subagent using `flash`. If it explicitly specifies `pro` (or `#pro`), use `pro`.
    2. **Complexity-Gated Evaluation:** When a plan or task includes a Complexity Score (`1–10`) or clear execution blueprint:
       - **Complexity `<= 6` (Clear blueprint, localized edits, unit tests, smoke checks):** Set `Model: 'flash'` and enforce the Single-Action Protocol.
       - **Complexity `> 6` (Complex multi-package architecture, ambiguous root causes):** Set `Model: 'pro'` / `'inherit'`.
    3. **Default / Fallback:** Default to `inherit` (strictly preserves the user's currently selected IDE model, e.g. Flash High, rather than overriding with a heavy model).
  - **Role:** `Artifact Executor`
  - **Prompt:** Keep the prompt extremely minimal. Do NOT pass the plan inline. Example: *"Read the execution plan located at `[Absolute Path to Artifact]` and implement all steps thoroughly. Follow the Single-Action Protocol from `flash/SKILL.md` (no `&&` or `;` command chaining, sequential verification). Verify whether any referenced rule file in `.agents/rules/` requires synchronization with the new exports/components, and include it in the final verification step. Do not stop until all steps are complete."*
- **Step 3: Handoff:**
  - Inform the user that the artifact has been generated and the subagent is currently running in the background.
  - You MUST provide a clickable link to the subagent's isolated session using the exact format: `[View Subagent Stream](conversation://<subagent-conversation-id>)`. Note: Live progress can also be monitored via the IDE's subagent status pill / background task panel.
  - Stop calling tools and wait for the subagent to report back.

================
File: .agents/skills/exec/SKILL.md
================
---
name: exec
description: >-
  Implement steps from the most recent plan.
  Triggered when the user uses the #exec tag or /exec command.
---

# Exec Workflow

- Implement step `N` (passed as an argument or in the prompt) from the most recent plan in the current conversation.
- **3-Tier Model Routing (Flash-First Strategy):** Inspect the plan's `Recommended Execution Model` and the per-step `[Score: X/10 | Tier: ...]` tag to select the optimal execution tier. Triggering `#exec` / `/exec` grants explicit user authorization to pass `Model: 'flash_lite' | 'flash' | 'pro'` to `invoke_subagent` (overriding default `'inherit'` in Mode A). All implementation subagents MUST use `TypeName: 'self'`:
  1. **Tier 1 — `FLASH_LITE` (Score 1–3, Trivial / Mechanical):** Route isolated copy, config, type, or CSS edits (<300 lines) to `flash_lite` (`invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'self'`, `Model: 'flash_lite'`, `Role: 'Step Executor'`) or execute inline under `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` per the Inline vs. Subagent Decision Rule below.
  2. **Tier 2 — `FLASH` (Score 4–6, Standard Scoped Step):** Default workhorse mode. Route well-bounded component/hook steps (<= 3–4 files, <500 lines) to `flash` (`invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'self'`, `Model: 'flash'`, `Role: 'Step Executor'`) or execute inline under `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` per the Inline vs. Subagent Decision Rule below.
  3. **Tier 3 — `PRO` (Score 7–10, Architectural / 500+ Line Files):** Route complex cross-file state refactors, SSR/Hydration logic, or files >=500 lines (`AGENTS.md` Mandatory Pro Decomposition) to a `pro` subagent (`invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'self'`, `Model: 'pro'`, `Role: 'Step Executor'`). When Score >= 8 or `Recommended Execution Model: PRO` applies to a high blast-radius change, enforce **Pro Swarm Execution (Red-Teaming)**: after the Implementer completes the changes, spawn a read-only auditor via `invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'research'`, `Model: 'pro'`, `Role: 'Red-Team Auditor'`, immediately output `[🛡️ Watch Red-Team Audit](conversation://<reviewer-conversation-id>)`, and require a compact 2-point audit report (`1. Verdict: [PASS | BLOCKING_ISSUES]`, `2. Findings: [Concise file:line bullets or "None"]`) _before_ finalizing the turn.
  4. **Hybrid Plans (`HYBRID`):** Execute `FLASH_LITE` and `FLASH` steps using Flash subagents, and escalate **only** the `PRO`-tagged step(s) to a `pro` subagent.
  - **Inline vs. Subagent Decision Rule:** Execute a Tier 1 (`FLASH_LITE`) or Tier 2 (`FLASH`) step **inline** under `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` when running a single step (`#exec N`) AND the active session model already matches (or is lighter than) the step's target tier. **Delegate via `invoke_subagent` (`TypeName: 'self'`)** when the active session model differs from the step's target tier (e.g., Coordinator is `Pro` and step is `FLASH`/`FLASH_LITE`, or Coordinator is `Flash` and step is `PRO`), or when executing multi-step plans (`#exec all`) where subagent isolation prevents context bloat.
  - **Subagent Context & Observability Contract:** Because spawned subagents start with a fresh conversation context and cannot see parent chat history, whenever delegating a step to a subagent, pass either the complete step specification (target file paths, required logic changes, and constraints) inside `Prompt` or the path to the saved plan artifact/file on disk. Forbid subagents from running `git commit`, enforce the standardized 3-point return format (`1. Status: [SUCCESS | BLOCKED | FAILED]`, `2. Files Modified: [Relative paths]`, `3. Summary: [1-2 sentences]`), and immediately emit `[⚡ Watch Step Stream](conversation://<subagent-conversation-id>)` in chat before waiting.
- **Auto-Escalation on Validation Failure (`FLASH` → `PRO`):** If a `flash_lite` or `flash` subagent/step produces a type, lint, or test failure that is not resolved after **one** targeted diagnostic and repair attempt, immediately escalate that step to a `pro` subagent (`TypeName: 'self'`, `Model: 'pro'`) instead of repeating failed Flash retries.
- **Pro Quota-Exhaustion & Graceful Fallback (`PRO` Quota / Rate-Limit Exceeded):** If a step requires `PRO` (or auto-escalates to `PRO`) but the Pro model quota is exhausted (Rate Limit / 429 / ResourceExhausted):
  1. Fall back to `FLASH` with strictly enforced Single-Action Protocol from `flash/SKILL.md`, targeted diagnostic logging, and micro-batched search/replace edits.
  2. If `FLASH` also cannot resolve the step after 2 attempts due to deep architectural limits (e.g., file > 500 lines or complex cross-domain state rewrites), execute a **Graceful Pause**: run `npm run lint` to verify working-tree integrity, preserve the clean intermediate state without rolling back, and alert the user: `[⚠️ Quota Fallback: PRO quota exhausted. Step paused gracefully at clean boundary. Please switch model or resume after quota cooldown.]`. Never enter an infinite retry loop.
- **Acceptance & Invariant Checklist Gate:** In addition to compilers and linters (`npm run lint`, `npm test`), the Executor must verify all items in the plan's `Acceptance & Invariant Checklist` point by point before finalizing the batch or turn.
- **Flash Execution Mode & Single-Action Protocol:** When executing under a Flash model (or if `#flash` or `Recommended Execution Model: FLASH` is present), strictly enforce the Single-Action Protocol from `flash/SKILL.md` (execute one state-changing command per step, never chain commands with `&&` or `;`, run diagnostic commands before attempting secondary fixes, and keep reasoning concise). Delegate isolated/repetitive file edits to a `flash` subagent to save time, or execute directly under these stability rules.
- **Multi-IDE Capability Translation:** Adapt subagent calls automatically to the active host IDE (see `.agents/rules/multi-ide-setup.md`):
  - **Mode A (Native Multi-Model Subagents, e.g., Antigravity):** Use `invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'self'` (or `'research'` for Red-Team audits) and `Model: 'flash_lite' | 'flash' | 'pro'`.
  - **Mode B (Fixed-Model Subagents / Rate-Limited Agents, e.g., Kimi Code / CLI):** Spawn `inherit` subagents for context isolation (respecting sequential rate limits in `KIMI.md` where applicable) and enforce `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` for Tier 1–2 steps.
  - **Mode C (Single-Agent IDEs without Subagents, e.g., Cursor Composer / Cline):** Execute Tier 1–2 steps directly under `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md`. Before a Tier 3 (`PRO`, Score >= 7) step, if running on a lightweight model, advise switching to the IDE's high-reasoning model; if already on a high-reasoning model, perform a dedicated self-review pass before committing.
- **Plan Continuity:** This refers strictly to plans present in the current conversation. Plans are not assumed to persist across unrelated sessions. For cross-session execution, the user must provide the plan again or explicitly authorize storing it as project documentation. If the referenced plan is unavailable or the requested step cannot be identified safely, ask for the plan instead of guessing.
- Apply the global preflight, scope, validation, and decision-gate contract in `AGENTS.md`; if new material ambiguity appears, pause and run `#grillme`.
- If `N` is `all`, implement all remaining steps in order without stopping for routine confirmations.
- Edit files directly rather than returning source code for manual copying.
- **Direct File Modification (No Code Blocks in Chat):** Apply all changes directly using file tools. Do NOT output code blocks, file headers, or source code in chat responses unless the user explicitly requests them.
- Preserve existing user changes and all protected behavior defined in project guidelines (like AGENTS.md if present).
- After every implemented step, check environment integrity, build configurations, and relevant platform behavior. Fix discovered regressions before continuing.
- Run the repository's relevant type-check, tests, or build when feasible and report the actual results.
- Stop for authorization only when an irreversible action, external side effect (e.g., `git push`, Cloudflare deployment), new authority, or material scope expansion is required.
- **Git & Checkpoint Policy (Intermediate Steps vs. Plan Completion):**
  - **Intermediate Steps (`#exec N` where subsequent steps remain):** Run step validation (`npm run lint`) and report status, leaving changes uncommitted without bumping `package.json` (unless an optional intermediate step checkpoint is explicitly requested).
  - **Plan Completion (`#exec all` or the final step of the plan):** Autonomously execute the following 6-point policy upon completing all planned steps (never leave the checkpoint commit unexecuted, pending, or deferred to the user):
  1. **Pre-Commit Verification:** Run type-check and linter (`npm run lint` or `npm run check`). If tests or type-checks fail, resolve them first or abort; never commit broken code. Ensure the patch version in `package.json` is incremented upon feature completion or code update.
  2. **Branch Check:** Prefer committing on development branches (`preview` or dedicated `feature/*`/`task/*` branches). If currently on `main` or `master`, output a clear warning, but do not hard-block if working directly on main or explicitly instructed.
  3. **Scope Inspection:** Run `git status --short` to inspect working tree status. If untracked or unexpected out-of-scope files exist, warn in the final summary, but proceed with staging only the explicitly planned files.
  4. **Explicit Staging:** Stage modified and planned files explicitly by relative path (`git add <file1> <file2>`). NEVER stage blindly with `git add .` or `git add -A`.
  5. **Conventional Commit:** Create an autonomous commit using Conventional Commits format (`type(scope): subject\n\nbody`) including a concise rationale in the commit body.
  6. **Remote Safety:** NEVER push automatically to remote (`git push` is an irreversible external action and a strict Hard Stop requiring explicit user instruction).
- **Final response:**
  - Summarize what changed and why in clear, concise bullet points.
  - List validation performed and any remaining risks.
  - Explicitly state the required refresh/deployment action needed from the user.
  - Do not reproduce source code or code blocks in the chat response.

================
File: .agents/skills/fetch-skill/SKILL.md
================
---
name: fetch-skill
description: >-
  Fetch, sanitize, and import external skill templates into the workspace.
  Triggered when the user requests finding, importing, or building a skill
  for a specific topic (e.g., "Find a skill for [topic]", "Import a skill for [topic]").
  Also triggered by #fetch-skill or /fetch-skill.
---

# Fetch-Skill Workflow

**Purpose:** Discover external skill templates, deep-transform them to match the project's tech stack and architectural conventions, and install them as first-class Antigravity skills in the workspace.

## Phase 1: Headless Discovery

1. **Search** for skill templates matching the user's topic using tool calls:
   - GitHub API or web search targeting `vercel-labs/skills`, `vercel-labs/agent-skills`, and `skills.sh` registry.
   - Broader web search for `"<topic> SKILL.md"` or `"<topic> agent skill template"` as fallback.
2. **Never** use interactive CLI tools like `npx skills` — these require TTY input and block the agent.
3. **Collect** up to 3 candidate results with name, source URL, and short description.

## Phase 2: Selection

- **Multiple matches (2+):** Present the top 3 candidates to the user with name, source, and one-line description. Let the user choose.
- **Single match:** Proceed directly with that result.
- **No match:** Inform the user that no suitable template was found and suggest refining the search terms or describing the desired skill manually.

## Phase 3: Raw Extraction

1. Read the raw content of the selected skill's `SKILL.md` directly from the web (GitHub raw URL or equivalent).
2. If the skill has a `references/` directory with additional documentation, note the file list but do not fetch unless needed for transformation.

## Phase 4: Deep Transform

Transform the extracted content into a clean Antigravity skill that matches the project's conventions:

1. **Strip LLM-specific boilerplate:**
   - Remove Claude XML tags (`<system>`, `<instructions>`, `<artifact>`, etc.).
   - Remove agent-specific CLI instructions (e.g., `claude mcp add`, `cursor settings`, `codex configure`).
   - Remove platform-specific installation steps that do not apply to Antigravity.

2. **Adapt to project tech stack:**
   - Derive the project's tech stack, architecture patterns, and coding conventions from the active project rules (`AGENTS.md` / `GEMINI.md`), which are already loaded into context.
   - Rewrite framework-specific instructions to match the project's actual stack (e.g., if the source assumes Next.js but the project uses Vite + React, adapt accordingly).
   - If no project rules are available (no active workspace or no `AGENTS.md`), import the skill generically without stack-specific adaptation and note this in the confirmation message.

3. **Rewrite into the project's skill style:**
   - Use clear, imperative bullet points for actionable instructions.
   - Remove philosophical preambles and generic advice the agent already knows.
   - Include validation/verification steps where appropriate.
   - Structure with numbered phases or steps for complex workflows.

4. **Generate valid YAML frontmatter (YAML Safe):**
   - `name`: lowercase, hyphenated, derived from the skill topic.
   - `description`: Always use folded block scalar (`description: >-`) or double quotes so `#tag` and `/command` triggers are never stripped as YAML comments. Include both `#<name>` and `/<name>`.

5. **Code Snippet & Import Coherence:**
   - If the template includes code snippets, type definitions, or boilerplate, never rely on blind LLM generation.
   - Ensure imports match actual workspace packages and follow valid TypeScript/JavaScript import syntax without hallucinated specifiers.

## Phase 5: Collision Check

- Check if `.agents/skills/<name>/` already exists in the workspace.
- **If it exists:** Abort immediately. Inform the user that a skill with this name already exists at the target path. Do not overwrite.
- **If clear:** Proceed to write.

## Phase 6: Write to Workspace & Auto-Register

1. Create the directory `.agents/skills/<name>/`.
2. Write the transformed content to `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`.
3. If the source had substantial reference documentation, create a `references/` subdirectory and write condensed reference files there.
4. Target is always the **workspace** (`.agents/skills/`) unless the user explicitly requests global installation (`~/.gemini/config/skills/`).
5. **Auto-Register in `.agents/WORKFLOWS.md`:** Append the new skill triggers to `.agents/WORKFLOWS.md`:
   `- #<name> (or /<name>): .agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`

## Phase 7: Validate

- Re-read the written `SKILL.md` file.
- Verify that YAML frontmatter contains valid `name` and un-truncated `description` fields.
- **AST / Import Validation on Code Assets:** If the skill generates runnable helper scripts (in `scripts/`) or code templates, run the project's deterministic terminal check (`npm run lint` or `tsc --noEmit`) to verify syntax and import integrity instead of trusting model output.
- If validation fails, fix the issues and re-write.

## Phase 8: Confirm

- Output a concise confirmation message stating:
  - Skill name and written target path.
  - Source it was derived from.
  - Stack adaptations applied (if any).
  - Any caveats or limitations.
- Do NOT reproduce the full skill content in chat.

---
name: flash
description: >-
  Enforce high-stability execution rules optimized for high-speed / Flash models.
  Triggered when the user uses the #flash tag or /flash command.
---

# Flash Workflow

- Enforce high-stability execution rules optimized for high-speed / Flash models when modifying files, running terminal tasks, or debugging. Act as a global modifier enforcing these rules even if used without a specific workflow command.
- **Single-Action Protocol:** Perform exactly one state-changing shell command per step. Wait for output/exit codes before proceeding. Concurrent independent file edits are permitted if they do not violate model context limits.
- **No Command Chaining:** Never invent and chain shell commands using `&&` or `;`. Execute steps sequentially. (Pre-defined standard npm/project scripts are exempt).
- **Diagnostic Requirement:** If a step fails, execute an explicit diagnostic command (e.g., inspecting files, logs, or git status) to trace the root cause before attempting a secondary fix.
- **Structured Reasoning:** Keep reasoning concise and structured (e.g. in `<thought>` tags) to prevent tool-suppression issues.
- Can be combined with execution requests (e.g., `#flash #exec 1` or `/flash /refactor`) to enforce stability during automated edits.

name: grillme
description: >-
  Relentlessly interview the user on every aspect of their idea, decision, or plan.
  Triggered when the user uses the #grillme, /grillme, #grill-me, or /grill-me command.
---

# Grillme Workflow

- Relentlessly interview the user on every aspect of their idea, decision, or plan until a complete mutual understanding is reached.
- Go through each branch of the decision tree and resolve dependencies sequentially. Provide a recommended answer for each question.
- **Question Logic & Formatting:**
  - *Dependent Questions:* If a question logically depends on the user's answer to a previous question, present it individually and wait for a response.
  - *Independent Questions:* If multiple questions do not depend on future answers, group them in a single message. Prefix each independent question with a clear index number in brackets (e.g., `[1]`, `[2]`) so the user can answer them selectively.
- If a fact can be discovered by inspecting the environment (files, tools, codebase), inspect it automatically instead of asking the user.
- The user retains all decision ownership: present choices individually or indexed, and wait for confirmation.
- Do not proceed with execution or finalizing plans until the user explicitly confirms that a shared understanding has been reached.

File: .agents/skills/plan/SKILL.md
================
---
name: plan
description: >-
  Inspect the relevant repository context and create a detailed plan before proposing changes.
  Triggered when the user uses the #plan tag or /plan command.
---

# Plan Workflow

- Inspect the relevant repository context before proposing changes.
- Do not modify files or execute state-changing actions.
- Follow the global preflight, scope, and decision-gate contract in `AGENTS.md`.
- **Interactive Ambiguity Check (Auto-Grill):** If any part of the request or requirements contains unresolved architecture/logic choices, high-risk trade-offs, or open dependencies, trigger the `/grillme` workflow first. Interview the user using indexed/sequential questions to resolve open choices before rendering the final plan.
- **Mandatory Execution Metadata Header:** Every generated plan (whether written to a file or output in chat) MUST start with this exact markdown block at the very top:

  ```markdown
  ## Execution Metadata

  - **Recommended Workflow:** [#exec | #exec-wave] (e.g. `#exec all` or `#exec-wave`)
  - **Recommended Execution Model:** [FLASH_LITE | FLASH | PRO | HYBRID (FLASH + PRO)]
  - **Flash-Only Viability:** [YES (Micro-Batched <= 6) | NO (Step X requires PRO: rationale)]
  - **Complexity Assessment:** [Short rationale, e.g. "Standard UI component update (Flash)" vs "Hybrid: 3 Flash UI batches + 1 Pro state refactor"]
  ```
  - **Workflow Selection Rules (`#exec` vs. `#exec-wave`):**
    - **Recommend `#exec` (or `#exec all`):** For linear, sequential implementations, single-batch updates, tightly coupled steps, or tasks touching <= 3–4 files where steps build directly on preceding changes.
    - **Recommend `#exec-wave`:** For multi-batch roadmaps (`plans/00_MASTER_ROADMAP.md` / `plans/Batch_*.md`) partitioned into serial phases and parallel waves with disjoint file sets (Scatter-Gather pattern), or when coordinator context isolation is critical.
  - **Flash-First Slicing Strategy & Flash-Only Viability:**
    - **Default Flash Partitioning:** Planners MUST by default cut architectures into small, low-context micro-batches/steps (<= 3–4 files, complexity score <= 6) so that execution can run entirely on fast, token-efficient `FLASH` models without context bloat or quality degradation.
    - **Enforced Flash Slicing (`#plan #flash`):** When `#flash` is combined with `#plan`, the planner is strictly required to decompose all tasks so that NO individual step or batch exceeds Score 6.
    - **`Flash-Only Viability: NO` Criteria:** Set to `NO` ONLY when architectural limits prevent Flash execution: e.g. target file >= 500 lines (`AGENTS.md` Mandatory Pro Decomposition), or an indivisible, high-risk state/lifecycle refactoring requiring holistic deep reasoning.
  - **3-Tier Model Recommendation Rules (Flash-First Strategy):** Default to Flash models for speed and token efficiency, escalating to `PRO` strictly where architectural complexity demands it:
    - **Tier 1 — `FLASH_LITE` (Score 1–3, Trivial / Mechanical):** Copy updates, simple type exports, static config tweaks, or minor Tailwind/CSS classes in isolated files (<300 lines).
    - **Tier 2 — `FLASH` (Score 4–6, Standard Scoped Batch):** Default workhorse tier. Component/hook updates across max. 3–4 files with clear interfaces and files <500 lines. Plans should be cut by default into small, low-context `FLASH` batches (complexity <= 6) so they can be implemented without context bloat.
    - **Tier 3 — `PRO` (Score 7–10, Complex / Architectural):** High-risk cross-file state refactors (Zustand, SSR/Hydration, Audio/Video lifecycles), files >=500 lines (`AGENTS.md` Mandatory Pro Decomposition), or massive blast-radius changes.
    - **Hybrid Plans (`HYBRID`):** When a large task contains both routine changes and a complex core, split the plan so routine steps/batches are tagged `[Tier: FLASH | Score: <=6]` and only the complex step/batch is tagged `[Tier: PRO | Score: >=7]`.
  - **Advise the Executor:** Whenever a plan or specific batch is tagged `PRO`, explicitly instruct `#exec` / `#exec-wave` to route that step/batch to a `pro` subagent (and spawn a `pro` Reviewer subagent for Red-Teaming when Score >= 8).

- **Mandatory Output Structure:** After the Metadata Header, you MUST strictly follow this 6-step structure, adapting to the language of the user's prompt (e.g., English for English prompts, German for German prompts):
  1. **Architecture & Logic (Architektur & Logik):** Create a logical step-by-step architecture compatible with the project constraints. Tag every individual step or batch header with its execution tier and score: `[Score: X/10 | Tier: FLASH_LITE | FLASH | PRO]`. For non-trivial work, include the global preflight findings, assumptions, gaps, fallbacks, and exit conditions. For state-changing plans (involving file modifications), the final planned step MUST explicitly define the validation and checkpoint commit adhering to the Git & Checkpoint Policy (Step 5). For read-only/investigative plans without code changes, conclude with the analytical findings or recommendations.
  2. **File Inventory (Datei-Inventar):** List all affected files with their relative paths. Whenever an orchestrator component or hook listed in `.agents/rules/core-react-state.md` or `.agents/rules/app-architecture.md` (or other rule file) is refactored, the plan generator must automatically append the relevant rule file to the plan's File Inventory (e.g. `Batch N: Sync .agents/rules/app-architecture.md`).
  3. **Risk & Complexity Check (Risiko- & Komplexitäts-Check):** Evaluate the complexity on a scale of 1-10 for a) each individual task/batch (assigning `FLASH_LITE` for 1–3, `FLASH` for 4–6, `PRO` for 7–10), b) the combined execution. Assess Flash-Only Viability: if any step exceeds score 6 or touches a file >=500 lines, mark `Flash-Only Viability: NO` and assign `Tier: PRO` to that specific step/batch while keeping all independent lower-score steps on `FLASH` / `FLASH_LITE`.
  4. **Optimization/Combination Hint (Optimierungshinweis):** Check if individual tasks can be combined in a way that their joint complexity remains manageable (<= 6 for `FLASH`). Provide a concrete recommendation for these step combinations. For parallel execution across multiple independent batches with disjoint files, recommend the Scatter-Gather pattern via `#exec-wave` (where `FLASH_LITE`, `FLASH`, and `PRO` subagents process disjoint batches in isolation without intermediate commits, and the Coordinator consolidates validation and commit). For linear or tightly coupled steps, recommend `#exec`.
  5. **Git & Checkpoint Policy (Git- & Checkpoint-Leitfaden):** Explicitly list the validation and commit guidelines for the executor:
     - **Pre-Commit Verification:** Run type-check/linter (`npm run lint` or `npm run check`); fix any failures before committing. Increment patch version in `package.json` on code updates.
     - **Branch Check:** Prefer committing on development branches (`preview` or dedicated `feature/*`/`task/*` branches). If on `main`/`master`, issue a clear warning, but do not hard-block if working directly on main or explicitly authorized.
     - **Scope Inspection:** Check `git status --short`. If untracked or unrelated files exist, warn in the summary, but proceed without staging foreign files.
     - **Path-Specific Staging:** Stage modified files explicitly by relative path (NEVER blind `git add .`).
     - **Commit Format:** Conventional Commits with a concise rationale in the commit body.
     - **Remote Safety:** NEVER push automatically to remote (`git push` is a strict Hard Stop requiring explicit user instruction).
  6. **Acceptance & Invariant Checklist (Audit-Kriterien für Flash):** Formulate 3–5 explicit, falsifiable invariants and acceptance criteria that a Flash model or verification pass can evaluate deterministically after implementation:
     - **Container- & System-Resilience:** e.g. every file/disk I/O (`fs`) guarded in `try/catch` for diskless cloud container compatibility.
     - **State-Lifecycle & Concurrency:** e.g. no duplicate mutations/pushes, ISO-8601 timestamps guaranteed, race-condition shields active.
     - **Data, Schema & API Contracts:** e.g. canonical field names, immutable caching headers, strictly defined MIME types and path conventions.
     - **Keep-Sets & Edge-Case Protection:** e.g. preservation of sibling poster files, untouched external CDN URLs, graceful fallbacks.

- Describe the implementation strategy, affected files, dependencies, validation, and meaningful risks.
- Keep the plan concrete enough that an implementation turn can follow it without rediscovering the design.
- Avoid code-level rewrites and fenced code blocks unless the user explicitly asks for them.
- Do not force a confirmation question when the user requested only a plan and all context is already clear, but naturally ask the user how they would like to proceed based on the plan.

---
name: refactor
description: >-
  Improve structure and maintainability without intentionally changing observable behavior.
  Triggered when the user uses the #refactor tag or /refactor command.
---

# Refactor Workflow

- Improve structure and maintainability without intentionally changing observable behavior.
- **Parallel Refactoring (Scatter-Gather):** When performing bulk edits across independent files, spawn multiple `flash` subagents concurrently. These subagents must ONLY perform read/edit file operations. They are strictly forbidden from running shell commands to prevent terminal lock contention. The main agent ("Coordinator") gathers the results and executes a single sequential type-check/build at the end.
- **Flash Execution Protocol:** Whenever executing under a Flash model or with `#flash`, strictly follow the Single-Action Protocol from `flash/SKILL.md` (no command chaining with `&&` or `;`, sequential verification, diagnostic check on error).
- **AST & Compiler Validation over LLM Trust:** Never rely on model assumptions that imports or types remain valid across modified files. Run a deterministic terminal check (`npm run lint` or `npm run check`) to establish a baseline before changes and verify strictly after each batch.
- **Diff Limits & Bottom-Up Dependency Traversal:** Never rewrite an entire codebase or broad cross-cutting layer in one prompt. Refactor incrementally module-by-module along the dependency tree in strict bottom-up order:
  1. Types, interfaces, and foundational utilities (`types/`, `utils/`)
  2. Core business logic, services, stores, and state managers (`store/`, `hooks/`)
  3. UI presentation components and routing layers
- Inspect callers, shared types, state ownership, and platform-specific behavior before editing.
- Prefer focused extractions with clear responsibilities over broad rewrites.
- Edit files directly, then run the deterministic terminal validation (`npm run lint` / `npm test`).
- If preserving behavior is impossible or requirements conflict, stop and explain the required behavioral decision before proceeding.
- Summarize structural changes, validation results, and any remaining risks without reproducing complete files.
- **Documentation Evaluation & Prompt:** First, evaluate the refactor's impact. If the changes were purely cosmetic, strictly localized, or did not alter the overall system architecture, skip this step. However, if the refactor introduced new architectural patterns, modified global state, altered cross-component logic, or changed dependencies: (1) Read `AGENTS.md` and any relevant `.agents/rules/` files to analyze the current guidelines. (2) Strictly initiate a `#grillme`-style interview. Use your analysis to ask the prompter highly targeted, contextual questions about if and how these new patterns should modify the existing documentation.

File: .agents/skills/repomix/SKILL.md
================
---
name: repomix
description: >-
  Pack and split the codebase into AI-friendly context chunks with a strict maximum limit of 130,000 characters per file, semantically partitioned by directory boundaries, followed by an automated character audit.
  Triggered by #repomix, /repomix, or requests to bundle or split the repository.
---

# Repomix Codebase Chunking & Bundler Workflow

Pack the repository into semantically partitioned chunks with a strict maximum limit of **130,000 characters per chunk** (`repomix-output/bundle.*.txt`), designed for LLM context windows, multi-agent analysis, or external AI platforms.

## Core Architecture & 130k Partitioning Strategy

1. **Semantic Grouping (Preserving Code Context):**
   Repomix uses top-level directory boundaries to group files logically. A single file or function is **never sliced down the middle** across chunks.
2. **Buffer Margin for 130,000 Characters:**
   In `repomix.config.json`, `splitOutput` is set to `120000` (bytes). This 10,000-character safety margin accounts for XML wrapping, file headers, directory trees, and token metrics, ensuring the final files remain strictly `<= 130,000 characters`.
3. **Automated Audit:**
   Every bundling run is immediately verified by `scripts/patches_and_tests/repomix-audit.ps1`, reporting the exact character count, byte size, and pass/fail status per chunk.

---

## Execution Protocols

### Option A: Standard Full-Repository Packaging (Interactive or Direct)

From PowerShell or Terminal:

```powershell
# Non-interactive execution (safe for agent automation, no pause prompts)
.\repomix.bat --non-interactive
```

Or execute via npm:

```powershell
npm run repomix -- --non-interactive
```

Or run via explicit CLI commands:

```powershell
# 1. Clean previous chunks
if (Test-Path repomix-output) { Remove-Item -Recurse -Force repomix-output\* }

# 2. Run Repomix using the calibrated repomix.config.json
npx --yes repomix

# 3. Execute strict character audit (verifies <= 130,000 characters)
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\patches_and_tests\repomix-audit.ps1
```

---

### Option B: Unified Agent Rules & Documentation Bundle

When you only need to pack agent instructions, workflows, project rules, and architecture docs into a single, compact XML file without source code:

```powershell
npm run repomix:agents
# or directly:
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\patches_and_tests\repomix-agents.ps1
```

This outputs `repomix-output.xml` containing `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/WORKFLOWS.md`, `.agents/rules/*.md`, `.agents/skills/**/*.md`, and related architectural documentation.

---

### Option C: Custom CLI Split Overrides

To override target chunk limits on the fly:

```powershell
# Split with a specific size limit (e.g. 100kb chunks)
npx --yes repomix --split-output 100kb --output "repomix-output/custom.txt"

# Token-optimized compressed mode with splitting
npx --yes repomix --split-output 120kb --compress --remove-comments --output "repomix-output/bundle-compressed.txt"
```

---

## Configuration Reference (`repomix.config.json`)

The workspace configuration controls the packaging behavior:

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yamadashy/repomix/main/repomix.config.schema.json",
  "output": {
    "filePath": "repomix-output/bundle.txt",
    "style": "xml",
    "splitOutput": 120000,
    "parsableStyle": true,
    "removeComments": false,
    "removeEmptyLines": false,
    "topFilesLength": 5
  },
  "ignore": {
    "useGitignore": true,
    "useDefaultPatterns": true,
    "customPatterns": [
      "scripts/patches_and_tests/**",
      "plans/**",
      "*.zip",
      "*bundle*.txt",
      "repomix-output/**",
      "repomix-output*"
    ]
  },
  "security": {
    "enableSecurityCheck": true
  }
}
```

---

## Verification & Audit Checklist

After running the bundler, always verify:
1. **File Count & Naming:** Check `repomix-output/` contains sequential files (`bundle.1.txt`, `bundle.2.txt`, ...).
2. **Character Count Gate:** Confirm the audit table in the terminal outputs `[OK] <= 130k` for all chunks.
3. **Context Integrity:** Ensure key files (such as `src/`, `server.ts`, etc.) are captured without truncation.
4. **Security Check:** Confirm no secrets, API keys, or `.env` entries were bundled.

# Review Workflow

- Audit the code in context without modifying it.
- Treat all applicable local project instructions (like `AGENTS.md`) as the authoritative review contract. Check the reviewed code against its protected behaviors, architecture boundaries, performance requirements, and implementation safeguards rather than limiting the review to generic code quality.
- Prioritize findings by severity: security, correctness, data loss, deployment, performance, maintainability, then style.
- For every finding, identify the affected file and location, explain the impact, and give a concise remediation direction.
- Check cross-system, browser, and device compatibility where relevant, including OS styling, browser behavior, responsive layouts, touch/mouse/hybrid input, dynamic viewport behavior, and capability-gated fallbacks.
- **Checklist-Driven Audit Mode (`#review #flash`):**
  When a review is triggered with an `Acceptance & Invariant Checklist` (from a plan or prompt), systematically audit each target file against every specified invariant (Point-by-Point Evaluation). Structure the audit report into:
  1. **Invariant Evaluation Table / Findings:** For each criterion, state `[PASS | FAIL | WARNING]` with file:line citations and root-cause analysis.
  2. **Targeted Polish / Remediation (when explicitly requested):** If the user requests fixing issues found during review (e.g. "Post-Audit Polish"), apply targeted fixes directly under the Single-Action Protocol from `flash/SKILL.md`, followed by sequential verification (`npm run lint`, `npm test`, `npm run build`).
- State clearly when no material issues are found and mention any validation limits.
- Small illustrative excerpts are allowed when necessary, but do not rewrite complete files.

================
File: .agents/skills/zip-clean/references/troubleshooting.md
================
# Troubleshooting & Edge Cases (`zip-clean`)

## 1. Locked Files
If an editor or background server has locked a file, PowerShell's `.NET ZipArchive` might raise an `IOException` (`The process cannot access the file because it is being used by another process`).
- **Fix:** Stop active dev servers (`npm run dev`) or test runners before running `zip-clean`.

## 2. Long Path Names on Windows
Windows path limits (MAX_PATH = 260 characters) can sometimes affect deep folder structures.
- `zip-clean.ps1` uses .NET `[System.IO.Compression.ZipFile]` which supports long paths natively when paths are normalized.
- If you encounter a MAX_PATH issue, specify an `-OutputPath` near the drive root (e.g., `C:\temp\archive.zip`).

## 3. Accidental Inclusion of the ZIP Itself
When creating an archive inside the repository folder (e.g., `Y:\repo\archive.zip`), a naive file search might attempt to zip `archive.zip` while it is being written, causing recursion or corruption.
- `zip-clean.ps1` automatically excludes `$resolvedOutput` by comparing absolute paths before archiving, preventing this issue.

## 4. Cross-Platform ZIP Separators
Windows standard paths use backslashes (`\`), but ZIP file specifications require entry keys to use forward slashes (`/`).
- `zip-clean.ps1` explicitly converts relative paths to `/` when creating archive entries:
  ```powershell
  $entryName = $rel.Replace('\', '/')
  ```
  This ensures compatibility when opening the resulting archive on Linux, macOS, Android, or cloud servers.

## 5. Secret Protection
`git ls-files --exclude-standard` strictly obeys `.gitignore`. However, if someone forgot to add a secret file (like a newly created `.env.local` that isn't ignored yet), it might get picked up.
- Use `-AdditionalExcludes @('*.env*', '*.pem', '*.key')` or add the file to `.gitignore` first.

================
File: .agents/skills/zip-clean/scripts/zip-clean.ps1
================
<#
.SYNOPSIS
    Generates a clean ZIP archive of the repository excluding all .gitignore files.

.DESCRIPTION
    Uses Git and .NET ZipArchive to build a clean ZIP distribution.
    By default, it respects .gitignore rules completely and excludes:
    - Anything ignored by .gitignore (e.g., node_modules, dist, .cache, .env, build artifacts)
    - The internal .git/ directory itself
    - Any destination *.zip files (preventing recursive inclusion)

.PARAMETER OutputPath
    The target ZIP file path. Defaults to <RepoRoot>/<RepoName>_v<Version>_<Timestamp>.zip.

.PARAMETER Mode
    Selection mode:
    - 'WorkingTree' (Default): Includes tracked files + untracked non-ignored files.
    - 'Head': Fast export of committed files at Git HEAD (via git archive or ls-tree).
    - 'TrackedOnly': Includes git-tracked files only, using current working-tree content.

.PARAMETER Prefix
    Optional root directory name inside the ZIP archive.

.PARAMETER AdditionalExcludes
    Additional glob patterns or filenames to exclude (e.g., @('*.zip', '*.tmp', '.env*')).

.PARAMETER DryRun
    If set, lists files that would be included and outputs file counts without creating the ZIP.

.PARAMETER Force
    Overwrite output ZIP if it already exists.

.EXAMPLE
    .\zip-clean.ps1
    Creates a timestamped zip in the repository root.

.EXAMPLE
    .\zip-clean.ps1 -OutputPath "C:\Backups\Portfolio.zip" -Force

.EXAMPLE
    .\zip-clean.ps1 -DryRun
#>

[CmdletBinding()]
param (
    [Parameter(Position = 0)]
    [string]$OutputPath,

    [Parameter()]
    [ValidateSet('WorkingTree', 'Head', 'TrackedOnly')]
    [string]$Mode = 'WorkingTree',

    [Parameter()]
    [string]$Prefix = '',

    [Parameter()]
    [string[]]$AdditionalExcludes = @('*.zip', '*.tmp'),

    [Parameter()]
    [switch]$DryRun,

    [Parameter()]
    [switch]$Force
)

Set-StrictMode -Off
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'

# 1. Locate repository root
$repoRootRaw = (git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>$null)
if (-not $repoRootRaw -or -not (Test-Path $repoRootRaw)) {
    $repoRoot = (Get-Item (Get-Location).Path).FullName
    Write-Warning "Not inside a detected git repository. Working in: $repoRoot"
} else {
    $repoRoot = (Get-Item $repoRootRaw).FullName
}

# 2. Extract repository name & optional version from package.json
$repoName = Split-Path $repoRoot -Leaf
$version = ""
$pkgJsonPath = Join-Path $repoRoot "package.json"
if (Test-Path $pkgJsonPath) {
    try {
        $pkg = Get-Content -Raw -Path $pkgJsonPath | ConvertFrom-Json
        if ($pkg.version) {
            $version = "v$($pkg.version)_"
        }
    } catch {
        # ignore parse errors
    }
}

# 3. Determine default OutputPath
$timestamp = Get-Date -Format "yyyyMMdd_HHmmss"
if (-not $OutputPath) {
    $zipFileName = "${repoName}_${version}${timestamp}.zip"
    $OutputPath = Join-Path $repoRoot $zipFileName
}

$resolvedOutput = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($OutputPath)
$outputFileName = [System.IO.Path]::GetFileName($resolvedOutput)

# 4. Handle existing output file
if ((Test-Path $resolvedOutput) -and -not $Force -and -not $DryRun) {
    throw "Target archive already exists: $resolvedOutput. Use -Force to overwrite."
}

Write-Host "==========================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host " Clean ZIP Generator (Excluding .gitignore)" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host "==========================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
Write-Host "Repository Root : $repoRoot"
Write-Host "Archive Mode    : $Mode"
Write-Host "Target Archive  : $resolvedOutput"
if ($Prefix) { Write-Host "Archive Prefix  : $Prefix" }

# 5. Gather file list respecting .gitignore
Push-Location $repoRoot
try {
    $files = @()
    if ($Mode -eq 'Head') {
        # Committed files at HEAD
        $files = git ls-tree -r --name-only HEAD 2>$null
    } elseif ($Mode -eq 'TrackedOnly') {
        # Tracked files (working tree state)
        $files = git ls-files --cached 2>$null
    } else {
        # WorkingTree mode: tracked + untracked non-ignored files
        $files = git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard 2>$null
    }

    if (-not $files -or $files.Count -eq 0) {
        Write-Warning "No files returned by git command. Checking directory fallback..."
        $files = Get-ChildItem -Recurse -File | ForEach-Object {
            $rel = Resolve-Path -Relative $_.FullName
            $rel.TrimStart('.\')
        }
    }
} finally {
    Pop-Location
}

# 6. Filter file list
$filteredFiles = [System.Collections.Generic.List[string]]::new()
$totalRawBytes = 0

foreach ($rel in $files) {
    if (-not $rel) { continue }

    # Normalize slashes for comparison
    $normalizedRel = $rel.Replace('/', '\')
    $fullPath = Join-Path $repoRoot $normalizedRel

    # Check existence
    if (-not (Test-Path $fullPath -PathType Leaf)) {
        continue
    }

    # Exclude target output file if within repository tree
    if ([string]::Equals($fullPath, $resolvedOutput, [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)) {
        continue
    }

    # Exclude additional patterns
    $fileName = [System.IO.Path]::GetFileName($fullPath)
    $skip = $false
    foreach ($pattern in $AdditionalExcludes) {
        if ($fileName -like $pattern -or $normalizedRel -like $pattern) {
            $skip = $true
            break
        }
    }
    if ($skip) { continue }

    $filteredFiles.Add($rel)
    $item = Get-Item $fullPath
    $totalRawBytes += $item.Length
}

$rawSizeMB = [math]::Round($totalRawBytes / 1MB, 2)
Write-Host "Matched Files   : $($filteredFiles.Count) files ($rawSizeMB MB uncompressed)" -ForegroundColor Green

# 7. Dry run check
if ($DryRun) {
    Write-Host "`n[DRY RUN] The following files would be packaged:" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    $displayLimit = [math]::Min($filteredFiles.Count, 25)
    for ($i = 0; $i -lt $displayLimit; $i++) {
        Write-Host "  + $($filteredFiles[$i])"
    }
    if ($filteredFiles.Count -gt 25) {
        Write-Host "  ... and $($filteredFiles.Count - 25) more files." -ForegroundColor DarkGray
    }
    Write-Host "`nDry run complete. No ZIP file was created." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    exit 0
}

# 8. Create ZIP archive using .NET ZipArchive
$sw = [System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew()

Add-Type -AssemblyName System.IO.Compression
Add-Type -AssemblyName System.IO.Compression.FileSystem

$parentDir = Split-Path $resolvedOutput -Parent
if ($parentDir -and -not (Test-Path $parentDir)) {
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $parentDir -Force | Out-Null
}

if (Test-Path $resolvedOutput) {
    Remove-Item $resolvedOutput -Force
}

$zip = [System.IO.Compression.ZipFile]::Open($resolvedOutput, [System.IO.Compression.ZipArchiveMode]::Create)

try {
    $count = 0
    foreach ($rel in $filteredFiles) {
        $fullPath = Join-Path $repoRoot $rel.Replace('/', '\')
        
        # Zip entry paths MUST use forward slashes for universal compatibility
        $entryName = $rel.Replace('\', '/')
        if ($Prefix) {
            $trimmedPrefix = $Prefix.TrimEnd('/')
            $entryName = "$trimmedPrefix/$entryName"
        }

        # Write to archive with optimal compression
        [System.IO.Compression.ZipFileExtensions]::CreateEntryFromFile(
            $zip,
            $fullPath,
            $entryName,
            [System.IO.Compression.CompressionLevel]::Optimal
        ) | Out-Null

        $count++
        if ($count % 50 -eq 0 -or $count -eq $filteredFiles.Count) {
            Write-Progress -Activity "Compressing clean archive" -Status "$count / $($filteredFiles.Count) files" -PercentComplete (($count / $filteredFiles.Count) * 100)
        }
    }
} finally {
    $zip.Dispose()
    Write-Progress -Activity "Compressing clean archive" -Completed
}

$sw.Stop()

# 9. Verify and output results
if (Test-Path $resolvedOutput) {
    $zipItem = Get-Item $resolvedOutput
    $zipSizeMB = [math]::Round($zipItem.Length / 1MB, 2)
    $ratio = if ($totalRawBytes -gt 0) { [math]::Round((1 - ($zipItem.Length / $totalRawBytes)) * 100, 1) } else { 0 }
    $elapsedSec = [math]::Round($sw.Elapsed.TotalSeconds, 2)

    Write-Host "`n==========================================" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host " ARCHIVE SUCCESSFULLY CREATED" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "==========================================" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "File:       $($zipItem.FullName)"
    Write-Host "Size:       $zipSizeMB MB (Saved $ratio% vs $rawSizeMB MB raw)"
    Write-Host "Files:      $count files packaged"
    Write-Host "Duration:   $elapsedSec seconds"
    Write-Host "==========================================" -ForegroundColor Green
} else {
    throw "Failed to create archive at $resolvedOutput."
}

File: .agents/skills/zip-clean/SKILL.md

---
name: zip-clean
description: >-
  Generate a clean ZIP archive of the repository or project directory, excluding all files and directories matched by .gitignore, .git metadata, and temporary files.
  Triggered by #zip-clean, /zip-clean, #export-zip, /export-zip, #zip, /zip, or when the user asks to zip or package the project without gitignored files.
---

# Clean ZIP Generator Workflow (`#zip-clean`)

**Purpose:** Package the repository or target project into a clean, distributable ZIP archive that strictly respects `.gitignore` rules, strips `.git/` version-control metadata, excludes destination `.zip` files, and prevents inclusion of build artifacts, dependencies (`node_modules`), secrets, or cache files.

---

## Capabilities & Modes

The skill provides three modes of operation via its bundled PowerShell script `scripts/zip-clean.ps1`:

| Mode | Command Flag | Inclusions | Best For |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **`WorkingTree`** *(Default)* | `-Mode WorkingTree` | Tracked files + newly added untracked non-ignored files | Sharing current work-in-progress, backups, code handoffs with active uncommitted changes |
| **`Head`** | `-Mode Head` | Strictly committed files at `HEAD` | Clean release packaging, tagged version snapshots |
| **`TrackedOnly`** | `-Mode TrackedOnly` | Tracked files only (reflecting current file content) | Excluding all untracked files while preserving uncommitted edits to tracked files |

---

## Execution Instructions

### Option 1: Automatic Agent Execution (Recommended)

When the user asks to create a zip or triggers `#zip-clean`:

1. **Resolve Destination Path & Naming:**
   - Default filename: `<RepoName>_<Version>_<Timestamp>.zip` in the repository root or parent directory.
   - If user specified a custom path (e.g., `dist/backup.zip` or desktop), resolve the absolute path.

2. **Execute the Bundled Script via PowerShell:**
   ```powershell
   powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .agents/skills/zip-clean/scripts/zip-clean.ps1 -Mode WorkingTree
   ```
   *Custom path example:*
   ```powershell
   powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .agents/skills/zip-clean/scripts/zip-clean.ps1 -OutputPath "C:\Backups\Portfolio_Clean.zip" -Force
   ```
   *Dry-run example:*
   ```powershell
   powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .agents/skills/zip-clean/scripts/zip-clean.ps1 -DryRun
   ```

3. **Verify Archive Integrity:**
   - Confirm file exists and size is greater than 0 bytes.
   - Verify that excluded paths (`node_modules`, `.git`, `.env`, `dist`) are absent from the archive entries.
   - Note file count, uncompressed size, compressed size, and elapsed time.

4. **Deliver Concise Summary:**
   Provide the user with:
   - Full path to the created `.zip` file
   - File size (in MB)
   - Number of files packaged
   - Compression ratio achieved

---

### Option 2: Quick Terminal / npm Command

Users can directly run from PowerShell:
```powershell
npm run zip
```
Or with custom arguments:
```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .agents/skills/zip-clean/scripts/zip-clean.ps1 -DryRun
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .agents/skills/zip-clean/scripts/zip-clean.ps1 -OutputPath "release.zip" -Mode Head -Force
```

---

### Option 3: Pure Git CLI Alternative (Committed HEAD only)

If running in environments without PowerShell access:
```bash
git archive -o "archive_HEAD.zip" HEAD
```
*Note: `git archive` only bundles committed files at HEAD and will omit uncommitted modifications or new untracked files.*

---

## Parameter Reference (`scripts/zip-clean.ps1`)

| Parameter | Type | Default | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `-OutputPath` | `string` | `<Repo>_v<Ver>_<Timestamp>.zip` | Target `.zip` path. Relative or absolute. |
| `-Mode` | `string` | `'WorkingTree'` | Selection mode: `'WorkingTree'`, `'Head'`, or `'TrackedOnly'`. |
| `-Prefix` | `string` | `''` | Optional root directory folder name inside the archive. |
| `-AdditionalExcludes` | `string[]` | `@('*.zip', '*.tmp')` | Extra glob patterns to exclude from packaging. |
| `-DryRun` | `switch` | `false` | Lists files and counts without writing any file to disk. |
| `-Force` | `switch` | `false` | Overwrites destination `.zip` if it already exists. |

---

## References

- Detailed edge cases and troubleshooting: [troubleshooting.md](./references/troubleshooting.md)
- Bundled automation script: [zip-clean.ps1](./scripts/zip-clean.ps1)

/**
 * Chunk-based UTF-8 to Base64 encoding.
 * Processes bytes in chunks (32KB) to prevent "Maximum call stack size exceeded" errors
 * that occur with large files when using String.fromCharCode.apply on large arrays.
 */
export function utf8ToBase64(str: string): string {
  const bytes = new TextEncoder().encode(str);
  const CHUNK_SIZE = 0x8000; // 32768 bytes
  let binary = '';
  for (let i = 0; i < bytes.length; i += CHUNK_SIZE) {
    const chunk = bytes.subarray(i, i + CHUNK_SIZE);
    binary += String.fromCharCode.apply(null, chunk as unknown as number[]);
  }
  return btoa(binary);
}

/**
 * Decodes Base64 string back to UTF-8 string safely.
 */
export function base64ToUtf8(base64: string): string {
  const binary = atob(base64);
  const bytes = new Uint8Array(binary.length);
  for (let i = 0; i < binary.length; i++) {
    bytes[i] = binary.charCodeAt(i);
  }
  return new TextDecoder().decode(bytes);
}

File: cf-gemini-git-bridge/src/github.ts

import type { GitHubCommitResult } from './types';

const GITHUB_GRAPHQL_ENDPOINT = 'https://api.github.com/graphql';

/**
 * Generic GitHub GraphQL executor.
 */
export async function executeGraphQL<T = any>(
  query: string,
  variables: Record<string, any>,
  token: string
): Promise<T> {
  const res = await fetch(GITHUB_GRAPHQL_ENDPOINT, {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'User-Agent': 'Cloudflare-Worker-gemini-git-bridge',
      'Authorization': `Bearer ${token}`,
      'Content-Type': 'application/json'
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables })
  });

  if (!res.ok) {
    const text = await res.text();
    throw new Error(`GitHub GraphQL HTTP error ${res.status} (${res.statusText}): ${text}`);
  }

  const json: any = await res.json();
  if (json.errors && json.errors.length > 0) {
    const msg = json.errors.map((e: any) => e.message).join(' | ');
    throw new Error(`GitHub GraphQL error: ${msg}`);
  }

  return json.data as T;
}

/**
 * Retrieves the current HEAD commit OID for a branch.
 */
export async function getBranchHeadOid(
  owner: string,
  repo: string,
  branch: string,
  token: string
): Promise<{ headOid: string; resolvedBranch: string }> {
  const qualifiedBranch = branch.startsWith('refs/heads/') ? branch : `refs/heads/${branch}`;

  const query = `
    query GetBranchHead($owner: String!, $repo: String!, $branch: String!) {
      repository(owner: $owner, name: $repo) {
        ref(qualifiedName: $branch) {
          name
          target {
            oid
          }
        }
        defaultBranchRef {
          name
          target {
            oid
          }
        }
      }
    }
  `;

  const data = await executeGraphQL<any>(query, { owner, repo, branch: qualifiedBranch }, token);
  const repository = data?.repository;

  if (!repository) {
    throw new Error(`Repository "${owner}/${repo}" was not found or is not accessible with the provided token.`);
  }

  if (repository.ref?.target?.oid) {
    return {
      headOid: repository.ref.target.oid,
      resolvedBranch: repository.ref.name
    };
  }

  // If specific branch not found, but branch was default/empty or fallback
  if (!branch && repository.defaultBranchRef?.target?.oid) {
    return {
      headOid: repository.defaultBranchRef.target.oid,
      resolvedBranch: repository.defaultBranchRef.name
    };
  }

  throw new Error(`Branch "${branch}" (or "${qualifiedBranch}") not found in repository "${owner}/${repo}".`);
}

/**
 * Fetches existing file content from GitHub using GraphQL Blob or REST raw content fallback.
 */
export async function fetchFileContent(
  owner: string,
  repo: string,
  branch: string,
  path: string,
  token: string
): Promise<string> {
  // 1. Try GraphQL Blob object expression
  const expression = `${branch}:${path}`;
  const query = `
    query GetFileBlob($owner: String!, $repo: String!, $expression: String!) {
      repository(owner: $owner, name: $repo) {
        object(expression: $expression) {
          ... on Blob {
            text
            isTruncated
          }
        }
      }
    }
  `;

  try {
    const data = await executeGraphQL<any>(query, { owner, repo, expression }, token);
    const blob = data?.repository?.object;
    if (blob && typeof blob.text === 'string' && !blob.isTruncated) {
      return blob.text;
    }
  } catch {
    // If GraphQL blob query failed, proceed to REST fallback
  }

  // 2. REST raw contents fallback
  const encodedPath = path.split('/').map(encodeURIComponent).join('/');
  const restUrl = `https://api.github.com/repos/${encodeURIComponent(owner)}/${encodeURIComponent(repo)}/contents/${encodedPath}?ref=${encodeURIComponent(branch)}`;
  const res = await fetch(restUrl, {
    headers: {
      'User-Agent': 'Cloudflare-Worker-gemini-git-bridge',
      'Authorization': `Bearer ${token}`,
      'Accept': 'application/vnd.github.raw+json'
    }
  });

  if (!res.ok) {
    throw new Error(`Failed to fetch file "${path}" from "${owner}/${repo}" on branch "${branch}": HTTP ${res.status}`);
  }

  return await res.text();
}

/**
 * Executes createCommitOnBranch mutation.
 */
export async function createCommitOnBranch(
  owner: string,
  repo: string,
  branch: string,
  expectedHeadOid: string,
  message: string,
  additions: { path: string; contents: string }[],
  deletions: { path: string }[],
  token: string
): Promise<GitHubCommitResult> {
  const mutation = `
    mutation CreateCommitOnBranch($input: CreateCommitOnBranchInput!) {
      createCommitOnBranch(input: $input) {
        commit {
          oid
          url
          messageHeadline
        }
      }
    }
  `;

  const headline = message.split('\n')[0].trim();
  const body = message.includes('\n') ? message.split('\n').slice(1).join('\n').trim() : undefined;

  const fileChanges: Record<string, any> = {};
  if (additions.length > 0) {
    fileChanges.additions = additions;
  }
  if (deletions.length > 0) {
    fileChanges.deletions = deletions;
  }

  const variables = {
    input: {
      branch: {
        repositoryNameWithOwner: `${owner}/${repo}`,
        branchName: branch
      },
      expectedHeadOid,
      message: {
        headline,
        body: body || null
      },
      fileChanges
    }
  };

  const data = await executeGraphQL<any>(mutation, variables, token);
  const commit = data?.createCommitOnBranch?.commit;

  if (!commit?.oid) {
    throw new Error('createCommitOnBranch did not return commit information.');
  }

  return {
    oid: commit.oid,
    url: commit.url,
    messageHeadline: commit.messageHeadline
  };
}

================
File: cf-gemini-git-bridge/src/index.ts
================
import type { Env, CommitRequest } from './types';
import { utf8ToBase64 } from './encoding';
import { resolveFileContent } from './patch';
import { getBranchHeadOid, fetchFileContent, createCommitOnBranch, executeGraphQL } from './github';

const CORS_HEADERS: Record<string, string> = {
  'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': '*',
  'Access-Control-Allow-Methods': 'GET, POST, OPTIONS',
  'Access-Control-Allow-Headers': 'Content-Type, Authorization, x-bridge-secret',
  'Access-Control-Max-Age': '86400',
};

function jsonResponse(data: any, status = 200): Response {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(data, null, 2), {
    status,
    headers: {
      ...CORS_HEADERS,
      'Content-Type': 'application/json'
    }
  });
}

function errorResponse(message: string, status = 400, details?: any): Response {
  return jsonResponse({
    success: false,
    error: message,
    ...(details ? { details } : {})
  }, status);
}

function verifyAuth(request: Request, env: Env): boolean {
  if (!env.BRIDGE_SECRET) {
    return false;
  }

  const customHeader = request.headers.get('x-bridge-secret');
  if (customHeader && customHeader === env.BRIDGE_SECRET) {
    return true;
  }

  const authHeader = request.headers.get('Authorization');
  if (authHeader) {
    const token = authHeader.replace(/^Bearer\s+/i, '').trim();
    if (token === env.BRIDGE_SECRET) {
      return true;
    }
  }

  return false;
}

export default {
  async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
    // 1. Handle CORS Preflight
    if (request.method === 'OPTIONS') {
      return new Response(null, {
        status: 204,
        headers: CORS_HEADERS
      });
    }

    const url = new URL(request.url);
    const path = url.pathname.replace(/\/+$/, '') || '/';

    // 2. Health check endpoint (public)
    if (request.method === 'GET' && (path === '/' || path === '/health')) {
      return jsonResponse({
        status: 'ok',
        service: 'gemini-git-bridge',
        version: '1.0.0',
        timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
        hasBridgeSecret: Boolean(env.BRIDGE_SECRET),
        hasGithubToken: Boolean(env.GITHUB_TOKEN)
      });
    }

    // 3. Authenticate all protected endpoints
    if (!verifyAuth(request, env)) {
      return errorResponse('Unauthorized: Invalid or missing BRIDGE_SECRET in Authorization or x-bridge-secret header', 401);
    }

    if (!env.GITHUB_TOKEN) {
      return errorResponse('Server configuration error: GITHUB_TOKEN secret is not set in Worker environment.', 500);
    }

    try {
      // 4. Commit / Push Relay Endpoint (Supports POST /push, /commit, /api/commit)
      if (request.method === 'POST' && (path === '/push' || path === '/commit' || path === '/api/commit' || path === '/')) {
        const body = (await request.json().catch(() => {
          throw new Error('Malformed JSON payload');
        })) as CommitRequest;

        // Parse owner and repo
        let owner = body.owner || '';
        let repo = body.repo || '';

        if (!owner && repo.includes('/')) {
          const parts = repo.split('/');
          owner = parts[0];
          repo = parts.slice(1).join('/');
        }

        if (!owner || !repo) {
          return errorResponse('Missing required fields: Repository must be specified as "owner/repo" or separate "owner" and "repo".');
        }

        const commitMessage = body.commitMessage || body.message;
        if (!commitMessage) {
          return errorResponse('Missing required field: "commitMessage" (or "message") must be specified.');
        }

        const targetBranch = body.targetBranch || body.branch || 'main';
        const fileChanges = body.changes || body.files || [];
        const deletions = body.deletions || [];

        if (fileChanges.length === 0 && deletions.length === 0) {
          return errorResponse('Commit must include at least one entry in "changes" or "files" or "deletions".');
        }

        // 4a. Resolve HEAD Commit OID for target branch to prevent race conditions
        const { headOid, resolvedBranch } = await getBranchHeadOid(owner, repo, targetBranch, env.GITHUB_TOKEN);

        // 4b. Resolve File Changes (Apply Search/Replace, Hard Guardrail, & encode to Base64)
        const additions: { path: string; contents: string }[] = [];

        for (const fileInput of fileChanges) {
          if (!fileInput.path) {
            throw new Error('Every file entry must contain a "path" property.');
          }

          const contentFetcher = async (filePath: string) => {
            return await fetchFileContent(owner, repo, resolvedBranch, filePath, env.GITHUB_TOKEN);
          };

          const resolvedText = await resolveFileContent(fileInput, contentFetcher);
          const encodedBase64 = utf8ToBase64(resolvedText);

          additions.push({
            path: fileInput.path,
            contents: encodedBase64
          });
        }

        // 4c. Format Deletions
        const formattedDeletions: { path: string }[] = deletions.map((del) => {
          return typeof del === 'string' ? { path: del } : { path: del.path };
        });

        // 4d. Execute createCommitOnBranch via GitHub GraphQL
        const commitResult = await createCommitOnBranch(
          owner,
          repo,
          resolvedBranch,
          headOid,
          commitMessage,
          additions,
          formattedDeletions,
          env.GITHUB_TOKEN
        );

        return jsonResponse({
          success: true,
          commit: commitResult,
          branch: resolvedBranch,
          repository: `${owner}/${repo}`,
          filesChanged: additions.length + formattedDeletions.length
        });
      }

      // 5. Raw GraphQL Proxy Endpoint
      if (request.method === 'POST' && path === '/graphql') {
        const body: any = await request.json().catch(() => {
          throw new Error('Malformed JSON payload');
        });

        if (!body.query) {
          return errorResponse('Missing required field: "query" in GraphQL request.');
        }

        const result = await executeGraphQL(body.query, body.variables || {}, env.GITHUB_TOKEN);
        return jsonResponse({ success: true, data: result });
      }

      return errorResponse(`Endpoint not found: ${request.method} ${path}`, 404);
    } catch (err: any) {
      console.error('Request processing error:', err);
      return errorResponse(err.message || 'Internal server error', 500, {
        stack: err.stack
      });
    }
  }
};

================
File: cf-gemini-git-bridge/src/patch.ts
================
import type { FileChangeInput, PatchItem } from './types';

/**
 * Hard Guardrail: Patterns indicating AI placeholder text that would truncate code.
 */
const LLM_PLACEHOLDER_PATTERNS = [
  /\/\/\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}/i,
  /#\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}/i,
  /\/\*\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}\s*\*\//i,
  /<!--\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}\s*-->/i,
  /\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of)\s*code\s*\.{3,}/i,
  /\/\/\s*\.{3,}\s*$/m,
];

/**
 * Validates that file content does NOT contain LLM placeholder comments.
 * Throws an error immediately if detected.
 */
export function validateNoPlaceholders(content: string, filePath: string): void {
  for (const pattern of LLM_PLACEHOLDER_PATTERNS) {
    if (pattern.test(content)) {
      throw new Error(
        `Hard Guardrail Rejection: File "${filePath}" contains forbidden LLM placeholder text (e.g. "// ... existing code ..."). Commit rejected to prevent accidental code truncation.`
      );
    }
  }
}

/**
 * Extracts <<<<<<< SEARCH ... ======= ... >>>>>>> REPLACE blocks from text.
 */
export function parseSearchReplaceBlocks(text: string): PatchItem[] {
  const blocks: PatchItem[] = [];
  const regex = /<{5,9}\s*(?:SEARCH|ORIGINAL|HEAD)?\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n={5,9}\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n>{5,9}\s*(?:REPLACE|MODIFIED|UPDATED)?/g;
  let match: RegExpExecArray | null;

  while ((match = regex.exec(text)) !== null) {
    blocks.push({
      search: match[1],
      replace: match[2]
    });
  }

  return blocks;
}

/**
 * Applies a single search & replace transformation to a file content string.
 * Handles exact matches, CRLF vs LF differences, and trimmed line matches.
 */
export function applySearchReplace(original: string, search: string, replace: string, filePath: string = ''): string {
  // 1. Direct match
  if (original.includes(search)) {
    return original.replace(search, replace);
  }

  // 2. Normalized newline match (CRLF -> LF)
  const normOriginal = original.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
  const normSearch = search.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
  const normReplace = replace.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');

  if (normOriginal.includes(normSearch)) {
    const updated = normOriginal.replace(normSearch, normReplace);
    if (original.includes('\r\n')) {
      return updated.replace(/\n/g, '\r\n');
    }
    return updated;
  }

  // 3. Relaxed line-by-line trimmed match
  const origLines = normOriginal.split('\n');
  const searchLines = normSearch.split('\n');

  let matchStart = -1;
  for (let i = 0; i <= origLines.length - searchLines.length; i++) {
    let matches = true;
    for (let j = 0; j < searchLines.length; j++) {
      if (origLines[i + j].trim() !== searchLines[j].trim()) {
        matches = false;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (matches) {
      matchStart = i;
      break;
    }
  }

  if (matchStart !== -1) {
    const before = origLines.slice(0, matchStart).join('\n');
    const after = origLines.slice(matchStart + searchLines.length).join('\n');
    const updated = (before ? before + '\n' : '') + normReplace + (after ? '\n' + after : '');
    if (original.includes('\r\n')) {
      return updated.replace(/\n/g, '\r\n');
    }
    return updated;
  }

  const snippet = search.length > 200 ? `${search.slice(0, 200)}...` : search;
  throw new Error(`Search block could not be resolved for file "${filePath}". Target snippet:\n${snippet}`);
}

/**
 * Resolves a FileChangeInput into full updated file content.
 * If the input specifies full content without patches, it validates against placeholders and returns it directly.
 * If search/replace patches or blocks are present, it fetches existing file content, applies them, and validates the result.
 */
export async function resolveFileContent(
  fileInput: FileChangeInput,
  existingContentFetcher: (path: string) => Promise<string>
): Promise<string> {
  const patchesToApply: PatchItem[] = [];

  // Check explicit single search/replace
  if (typeof fileInput.search === 'string' && typeof fileInput.replace === 'string') {
    patchesToApply.push({ search: fileInput.search, replace: fileInput.replace });
  }

  // Check array of patches
  if (Array.isArray(fileInput.patches)) {
    for (const p of fileInput.patches) {
      if (typeof p.search === 'string' && typeof p.replace === 'string') {
        patchesToApply.push(p);
      }
    }
  }

  // Check diff or content for <<<<<<< SEARCH blocks
  const diffToCheck = fileInput.diff || fileInput.content || '';
  if (diffToCheck.includes('<<<<<<<') || diffToCheck.includes('=====')) {
    const parsed = parseSearchReplaceBlocks(diffToCheck);
    patchesToApply.push(...parsed);
  }

  // If no search/replace patches found, treat as full file content replacement/creation
  if (patchesToApply.length === 0) {
    if (typeof fileInput.content === 'string') {
      validateNoPlaceholders(fileInput.content, fileInput.path);
      return fileInput.content;
    }
    throw new Error(`File change for "${fileInput.path}" must provide either "content" or search/replace patches.`);
  }

  // Validate that replace blocks do not contain placeholders
  for (const patch of patchesToApply) {
    validateNoPlaceholders(patch.replace, fileInput.path);
  }

  // Fetch existing content
  const existingContent = await existingContentFetcher(fileInput.path);
  let resolved = existingContent;

  for (const patch of patchesToApply) {
    resolved = applySearchReplace(resolved, patch.search, patch.replace, fileInput.path);
  }

  // Validate the final resolved file
  validateNoPlaceholders(resolved, fileInput.path);

  return resolved;
}
