import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'url';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = path.dirname(__filename);
const ROOT_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, '../..');
const WORKER_DIST = path.join(ROOT_DIR, 'packages/edge-worker/dist/worker.mjs');

console.log('=== Stufe III: Funktionaler Test des Edge-Worker GitHub Webhooks (POST /api/webhook/github) ===\n');

let passCount = 0;
let totalCount = 0;

function assert(condition, message) {
  totalCount++;
  if (!condition) {
    console.error(`  ❌ [FAIL] ${message}`);
    throw new Error(`Assertion failed: ${message}`);
  }
  console.log(`  ✅ [PASS] ${message}`);
  passCount++;
}

// 1. Dynamic import of bundled worker
const workerModule = await import(pathToFileURL(WORKER_DIST).href);
const worker = workerModule.default;

assert(typeof worker?.fetch === 'function', 'Worker default export has fetch handler');

const TEST_SECRET = 'gh_webhook_secret_super_secure_98765';
const TEST_ENV = {
  GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET: TEST_SECRET,
  WORKER_SECRET: 'bridge-secret-12345',
  GITHUB_TOKEN: 'fake-gh-token'
};

// Helper to compute HMAC-SHA256 signature
async function computeSignature(payloadString, secret) {
  const enc = new TextEncoder();
  const key = await crypto.subtle.importKey(
    'raw',
    enc.encode(secret),
    { name: 'HMAC', hash: 'SHA-256' },
    false,
    ['sign']
  );
  const sig = await crypto.subtle.sign('HMAC', key, enc.encode(payloadString));
  const hex = Array.from(new Uint8Array(sig))
    .map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0'))
    .join('');
  return `sha256=${hex}`;
}

// 2. Test HMAC computation helper
const testBody = JSON.stringify({ action: 'created', comment: { body: 'Hello world' } });
const validSignature = await computeSignature(testBody, TEST_SECRET);
assert(validSignature.startsWith('sha256=') && validSignature.length === 71, 'HMAC-SHA256 signature format matches GitHub specification (71 chars)');

// 3. Test CORS Preflight OPTIONS /api/webhook/github
{
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'OPTIONS',
    headers: { 'Origin': 'https://github.com' }
  });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 204, 'OPTIONS /api/webhook/github returns 204 No Content for CORS preflight');
  assert(res.headers.get('Access-Control-Allow-Origin') === '*', 'CORS Allow-Origin is *');
}

// 4. Test Invalid HTTP Method (GET instead of POST)
{
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', { method: 'GET' });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 405, 'GET /api/webhook/github returns 405 Method Not Allowed');
}

// 5. Test Missing Signature Header (Security Guardrail)
{
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: testBody
  });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 401, 'POST without x-hub-signature-256 returns 401 Unauthorized');
  const data = await res.json();
  assert(data.error?.includes('HMAC signature'), 'Error response explicitly cites HMAC signature missing');
}

// 6. Test Wrong Secret / Tampered Payload (Security Guardrail)
{
  const wrongSignature = await computeSignature(testBody, 'completely-wrong-secret');
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'x-hub-signature-256': wrongSignature
    },
    body: testBody
  });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 401, 'POST with mismatched HMAC signature returns 401 Unauthorized');
}

// 7. Test Tampered Body
{
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'x-hub-signature-256': validSignature
    },
    body: testBody + 'tampered'
  });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 401, 'POST with tampered payload content returns 401 Unauthorized');
}

// 8. Test Malformed JSON Body
{
  const malformed = '{ invalid-json-syntax';
  const malformedSig = await computeSignature(malformed, TEST_SECRET);
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'x-hub-signature-256': malformedSig
    },
    body: malformed
  });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 400, 'POST with malformed JSON body returns 400 Bad Request');
}

// 9. Test Valid Signature without @gemini Trigger
{
  const nonGeminiPayload = JSON.stringify({
    action: 'created',
    issue: { number: 42, comments_url: 'https://api.github.com/repos/owner/repo/issues/42/comments' },
    comment: { body: 'Just a regular developer comment without mentioning the bot' }
  });
  const sig = await computeSignature(nonGeminiPayload, TEST_SECRET);
  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'x-hub-signature-256': sig
    },
    body: nonGeminiPayload
  });
  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV);
  assert(res.status === 200, 'POST with valid HMAC but without @gemini returns 200 OK');
  const data = await res.json();
  assert(data.status === 'IGNORED', 'Response status is IGNORED');
  assert(data.message?.includes('No @gemini trigger'), 'Message specifies absence of @gemini trigger');
}

// 10. Test Valid Signature WITH @gemini Trigger (P1 Async 202 Accepted & ctx.waitUntil)
{
  const geminiPayload = JSON.stringify({
    action: 'created',
    repository: { full_name: 'sikanda/test-repo', default_branch: 'main' },
    issue: { number: 101, comments_url: 'https://api.github.com/repos/sikanda/test-repo/issues/101/comments' },
    comment: { body: '@gemini please patch authentication handler to use bcrypt' }
  });
  const sig = await computeSignature(geminiPayload, TEST_SECRET);

  let waitUntilInvoked = false;
  let scheduledPromise = null;
  const mockCtx = {
    waitUntil: (p) => {
      waitUntilInvoked = true;
      scheduledPromise = p;
    }
  };

  const req = new Request('https://worker.local/api/webhook/github', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'x-hub-signature-256': sig
    },
    body: geminiPayload
  });

  const res = await worker.fetch(req, TEST_ENV, mockCtx);
  assert(res.status === 202, 'POST with valid @gemini webhook immediately returns HTTP 202 Accepted');
  const data = await res.json();
  assert(data.status === 'ACCEPTED', 'Response status is ACCEPTED');
  assert(data.message?.includes('execution scheduled'), 'Response message confirms execution scheduled');
  assert(waitUntilInvoked === true, 'P1 Invariant: Worker calls ctx.waitUntil() to decouple background execution from 10s webhook response');
  assert(scheduledPromise instanceof Promise, 'Background workflow task is a valid Promise');
}

console.log(`\n🎉 Stufe III Test abgeschlossen: ${passCount}/${totalCount} Assertions bestanden!`);

import type { WorkerEnv } from './types';

// ==========================================
// 1. EXECUTION MODE & HYBRID ROUTING
// ==========================================
export type ExecutionMode = 'local' | 'worker';

export interface HybridTaskInput {
  prompt: string;
  repo: string;                 // Format: "owner/repository"
  branch: string;               // e.g. "main"
  targetPaths?: string[];       // Subpath filters or files to include
  issueOrPrNumber?: number;     // Optional issue/PR number for status commenting
}

export interface HybridTaskResult {
  mode: ExecutionMode;
  status: 'SUCCESS' | 'PREFLIGHT_FAILED' | 'ERROR';
  commitSha?: string;
  errors?: any[];
  message?: string;
}

// ==========================================
// 2. HYBRID WORKER ENVIRONMENT CONTRACTS
// ==========================================
export interface HybridWorkerEnv extends WorkerEnv {
  EXECUTION_MODE?: ExecutionMode;
  ENVIRONMENT?: string;
  GEMINI_API_KEY: string;
  GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET: string;
}

// ==========================================
// 3. GITHUB WEBHOOK CONTRACTS (Pure Webhook Events)
// ==========================================
export interface GitHubWebhookSender {
  login: string;
  id?: number;
  type?: string;
}

export interface GitHubWebhookRepository {
  name: string;
  full_name: string;
  owner: {
    login: string;
    id?: number;
  };
  default_branch?: string;
  private?: boolean;
  html_url?: string;
}

export interface GitHubWebhookIssue {
  number: number;
  title: string;
  body?: string | null;
  comments_url: string;
  html_url: string;
  user?: GitHubWebhookSender;
}

export interface GitHubWebhookPullRequest {
  number: number;
  title: string;
  body?: string | null;
  comments_url: string;
  html_url: string;
  head?: {
    ref: string;
    sha: string;
  };
  base?: {
    ref: string;
    sha: string;
  };
  user?: GitHubWebhookSender;
}

export interface GitHubWebhookComment {
  id: number;
  body: string;
  user: GitHubWebhookSender;
  html_url?: string;
  created_at?: string;
}

export interface GitHubWebhookPayload {
  action?: string;
  issue?: GitHubWebhookIssue;
  pull_request?: GitHubWebhookPullRequest;
  comment?: GitHubWebhookComment;
  repository?: GitHubWebhookRepository;
  sender?: GitHubWebhookSender;
}

# ==============================================================================
# GEMINI PROMPT TEMPLATES & QUICK SNIPPETS - GEMINI-TO-GITHUB BRIDGE
# Datei: gemini_prompt_instructions.txt
# ==============================================================================

# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. SCHNELLE EINZEILER FÜR DEN CHAT (ZERO CONTEXT-BLOAT)
# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Diese Einzeiler kannst du einfach an das Ende deiner Prompt-Anfrage hängen,
# ohne jedes Mal lange Schemas oder Beispiele mitkopieren zu müssen:

# [Empfehlung: Atomares Multi-File JSON]
Gib die Änderungen ausschließlich als Patch-JSON mit branch, message und files (search/replace) aus.

# [Alternative: Klassisches Search/Replace]
Gib die Änderung als SEARCH/REPLACE Block mit // filepath: an.

# [English Quick Triggers]
Output changes strictly as Patch-JSON with branch, message, and files (search/replace).
Output changes as SEARCH/REPLACE blocks with // filepath: headers.


# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. DEUTSCHE VOLLVERSION (Für Gemini System Instructions / Prompt-Presets)
# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Wenn du Code änderst oder neue Dateien erstellst, beachte strikt folgende Regeln für die GitHub-Bridge:

REGEL 1: FORMAT-AUSWAHL
Verwende bevorzugt das strukturierte V2 Patch-JSON (besonders bei Änderungen über mehrere Dateien hinweg).
Alternativ kannst du bei einzelnen Dateien SEARCH/REPLACE Blöcke verwenden.

--- FORMAT A: V2 PATCH-JSON (EMPFOHLEN) ---
Gib die Antwort als valides JSON in einem ```json Codeblock aus:
{
  "branch": "main",
  "message": "feat: kurze beschreibung der aenderung",
  "files": [
    {
      "path": "relativer/pfad/zur/datei.ts",
      "type": "modify",
      "search": "// exakter bisheriger Codeausschnitt aus dem Repo",
      "replace": "// neuer ersetzter Codeausschnitt"
    },
    {
      "path": "relativer/pfad/zu/neuer_datei.ts",
      "type": "create",
      "content": "// vollstaendiger neuer Dateiinhalt ohne Kuerzungen"
    },
    {
      "path": "relativer/pfad/zu/alter_datei.ts",
      "type": "delete"
    }
  ]
}

--- FORMAT B: SEARCH / REPLACE BLÖCKE (KLASSISCH) ---
Gib in der allerersten Zeile jedes Blocks immer den relativen Dateipfad an:
// filepath: relativer/pfad/zur/datei.ts
<<<<<<< SEARCH
exakter_alter_code_aus_dem_repo
=======
neuer_ersetzter_code
>>>>>>> REPLACE

Für komplett neue oder vollständig ersetzte Dateien:
// filepath: relativer/pfad/zur/neuen_datei.ts
// Vollständiger Code ohne Kürzungen

--- STRIKTE GUARDRAIL (KEINE PLATZHALTER) ---
Verwende NIEMALS Platzhalter wie "// ... existing code ...", "/* ... rest of code ... */" oder "# ... remaining code".
Der Server lehnt solche Commits automatisch mit "400 Bad Request" ab, um versehentlichen Datenverlust zu verhindern. Wenn du nicht die gesamte Datei ausgeben willst, verwende immer Search/Replace!


# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. ENGLISH FULL VERSION (For Gemini System Instructions / Prompt Presets)
# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When modifying code or creating new files, strictly adhere to these rules for the GitHub Bridge:

RULE 1: FORMAT SELECTION
Prefer V2 Patch-JSON (especially for changes spanning multiple files in a single atomic commit).
Alternatively, use SEARCH/REPLACE blocks for single-file edits.

--- FORMAT A: V2 PATCH-JSON (RECOMMENDED) ---
Output the patch as valid JSON within a ```json code block:
{
  "branch": "main",
  "message": "feat: brief description of change",
  "files": [
    {
      "path": "relative/path/to/file.ts",
      "type": "modify",
      "search": "// exact existing snippet from repo",
      "replace": "// replacement code snippet"
    },
    {
      "path": "relative/path/to/new_file.ts",
      "type": "create",
      "content": "// complete new file content without omissions"
    },
    {
      "path": "relative/path/to/deprecated_file.ts",
      "type": "delete"
    }
  ]
}

--- FORMAT B: SEARCH / REPLACE BLOCKS (CLASSIC) ---
Always specify the relative file path on the very first line of each block:
// filepath: relative/path/to/file.ts
<<<<<<< SEARCH
exact_existing_code_from_repo
=======
new_replacement_code
>>>>>>> REPLACE

For new or completely replaced files:
// filepath: relative/path/to/new_file.ts
// Complete code without truncations

--- STRICT GUARDRAIL (NO PLACEHOLDERS) ---
NEVER use comments like "// ... existing code ...", "/* ... rest of code ... */", or "# ... remaining code".
The edge worker automatically rejects commits with placeholders (400 Bad Request) to prevent irreversible code loss. If you do not want to output the entire file, always use Search/Replace!

# Gemini-to-GitHub Bridge (.info)

Universelle Edge-Brücke und Browser-Integration für atomare 1-Click-Git-Commits und Search/Replace-Patches direkt aus **Google Gemini** (`gemini.google.com`) und **Google AI Studio** (`aistudio.google.com`) in beliebige GitHub-Repositories.

---

## 1. Installation im Browser

Um die 1-Click-Commit-Buttons in Google Gemini und Google AI Studio zu nutzen:

1. **Tampermonkey** oder **Violentmonkey** im Browser öffnen.
2. **Neues Skript erstellen**.
3. **Inhalt von `gemini-bridge.user.js` hineinkopieren und speichern.**

### Konfiguration im Browser:
- Nach der Installation erscheint auf `gemini.google.com` und `aistudio.google.com` ein Einstellungs-Icon bzw. Bridge-Badge.
- Hier können die **Worker-URL** (Standard: `https://gemini-git-bridge.sikanda.workers.dev`), das **Bridge-Secret**, das **Standard-Repository** (`owner/repo`) und der **Standard-Branch** (z. B. `preview` oder `main`) hinterlegt werden.

---

## 2. Cloudflare Worker Setup & Deployment

Der Worker-Code liegt im Unterverzeichnis `cf-gemini-git-bridge/`.

### Schnellbefehle (Wrangler CLI)
Vor der Ausführung immer in das Verzeichnis wechseln:
```bash
cd cf-gemini-git-bridge
```

- **Abhängigkeiten installieren:** `npm install`
- **Lokaler Test:** `npx wrangler dev`
- **Live Deployment:** `npx wrangler deploy`
- **GitHub Token hinterlegen:** `npx wrangler secret put GITHUB_TOKEN`
- **Bridge Secret hinterlegen:** `npx wrangler secret put BRIDGE_SECRET`

### Credentials (Cloudflare Secrets)
- `GITHUB_TOKEN`: Fine-Grained Personal Access Token (Permissions: `Contents: Read and write`)
- `BRIDGE_SECRET`: Shared Secret zur Authentifizierung der API-Calls (`Authorization: Bearer <token>` oder `x-bridge-secret: <token>`)

---

## 3. Endpunkte & Nutzung

- `GET /health` (oder `GET /`): Öffentlicher Status-Check (prüft, ob Worker läuft und Secrets gesetzt sind).
- `POST /push` (oder `/commit`): Führt atomare Commits und Search/Replace-Patches aus.
- `POST /graphql`: Authentifizierter Proxy für freie GitHub GraphQL Abfragen.

---

## 4. Schutz vor Code-Kürzungen (Hard Guardrail)

Der Worker lehnt Commits automatisch ab (`400 Bad Request`), falls die Patches oder Dateiinhalte Platzhalter wie `// ... existing code ...` oder `/* ... rest of code ... */` enthalten. Damit wird verhindert, dass unvollständiger KI-Code bestehende Dateien zerstört.

For multi-stage orchestration progress and crash recovery, read `plans/STATE.md` and the last 25 lines of `plans/EXECUTION_LOG.md`.

# Gemini-to-GitHub Bridge

Universelle Edge-Brücke und Browser-Integration für atomare 1-Click-Git-Commits und Search/Replace-Patches direkt aus **Google Gemini** (`gemini.google.com`) und **Google AI Studio** (`aistudio.google.com`) in beliebige GitHub-Repositories.

---

## Architektur-Überblick

Das Projekt besteht aus zwei perfekt aufeinander abgestimmten Komponenten:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Browser (gemini.google.com / aistudio) │
│  └─ Tampermonkey: gemini-bridge.user.js │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                     │ HTTPS (JSON + x-bridge-secret)
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Cloudflare Worker (cf-gemini-git-bridge)│
│  ├─ Auth & Health Check                 │
│  ├─ Hard Guardrails (Anti-Placeholder)  │
│  ├─ In-Memory Search/Replace Resolver   │
│  └─ GitHub GraphQL API v4               │
└────────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                     │ createCommitOnBranch (GraphQL)
                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ GitHub Repository (Beliebige Branches)  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

1. **Frontend-Layer (`gemini-bridge.user.js`)**: Userscript für Tampermonkey/Violentmonkey. Fügt Code-Blöcken und Chat-Antworten einen interaktiven „Commit to GitHub“-Button mit Diff-Vorschau und Branch-Auswahl hinzu.
2. **Backend-Layer (`cf-gemini-git-bridge/`)**: Serverless Cloudflare Worker. Validiert Secrets, prüft HEAD-OIDs (Race-Condition-Schutz), löst Patches gegen GitHub-Blobs auf, filtert LLM-Platzhalter (`// ... existing code ...`) heraus und führt atomare Commits via GitHub GraphQL API v4 durch.

---

## 1. Installation im Browser (Userscript)

Um die 1-Click-Commit-Buttons in Google Gemini und AI Studio zu aktivieren:

1. **Tampermonkey** oder **Violentmonkey** im Browser öffnen (falls noch nicht vorhanden, über den Browser-Extension-Store installieren).
2. **Neues Skript erstellen** (im Dashboard der Extension auf `+` bzw. `Neues Skript hinzufügen` klicken).
3. **Inhalt von [`gemini-bridge.user.js`](gemini-bridge.user.js) hineinkopieren und speichern** (`Strg + S`).

### Konfiguration im Browser
Nach der Installation erscheint auf `gemini.google.com` und `aistudio.google.com`:
- Ein Einstellungs-Icon (Zahnrad / Bridge-Badge) unten rechts oder am Code-Block.
- Dort können die **Worker-URL**, das **Bridge-Secret**, das **Standard-Repository** (z. B. `owner/repo`) und der **Standard-Branch** (z. B. `main` oder `preview`) konfiguriert werden.

---

## 2. Cloudflare Worker Setup & Deployment

Der Worker befindet sich im Unterverzeichnis `cf-gemini-git-bridge/`.

### Schnellbefehle (Wrangler CLI)

Vor dem Ausführen von Befehlen immer in den Worker-Ordner wechseln:

```bash
cd cf-gemini-git-bridge
```

| Aktion | Befehl |
|---|---|
| Abhängigkeiten installieren | `npm install` |
| Lokaler Test | `npx wrangler dev` |
| Live Deployment | `npx wrangler deploy` |
| GitHub Token hinterlegen | `npx wrangler secret put GITHUB_TOKEN` |
| Bridge Secret hinterlegen | `npx wrangler secret put BRIDGE_SECRET` |

### Benötigte Secrets (Cloudflare Worker)
- **`GITHUB_TOKEN`**: Fine-Grained Personal Access Token (PAT) von GitHub mit der Berechtigung `Contents: Read and write` für die Ziel-Repositories.
- **`BRIDGE_SECRET`**: Frei wählbares Secret (z. B. 64-stelliger Hex-Token), das zur Absicherung der Worker-API dient.

---

## 3. API-Endpunkte & Authentifizierung

Der Worker akzeptiert Authentifizierung über:
- Header: `Authorization: Bearer <BRIDGE_SECRET>`
- Header: `x-bridge-secret: <BRIDGE_SECRET>`

### 1. Health-Check (öffentlich)
- **Methode:** `GET /` oder `GET /health`
- **Antwort:**
```json
{
  "status": "ok",
  "service": "gemini-git-bridge",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "timestamp": "2026-10-15T14:30:00.000Z",
  "hasBridgeSecret": true,
  "hasGithubToken": true
}
```

### 2. Commit Relay (geschützt)
- **Methode:** `POST /push`, `POST /commit`, `POST /api/commit` oder `POST /`
- **Beispiel-Payload (Search/Replace Patch):**
```json
{
  "repo": "meinUser/meinRepo",
  "targetBranch": "main",
  "commitMessage": "fix: update button styles",
  "changes": [
    {
      "path": "src/styles.css",
      "search": "background: red;",
      "replace": "background: #2563eb;"
    }
  ]
}
```
- **Beispiel-Payload (Ganze Datei anlegen oder überschreiben):**
```json
{
  "repo": "meinUser/meinRepo",
  "targetBranch": "feature/login",
  "commitMessage": "feat: add config file",
  "changes": [
    {
      "path": "config.json",
      "content": "{\n  \"apiUrl\": \"https://api.example.com\"\n}"
    }
  ]
}
```
- **Beispiel-Payload (Datei löschen):**
```json
{
  "repo": "meinUser/meinRepo",
  "targetBranch": "main",
  "commitMessage": "chore: remove deprecated file",
  "deletions": [
    { "path": "old-file.txt" }
  ]
}
```

### 3. Raw GraphQL Proxy (geschützt)
- **Methode:** `POST /graphql`
- Leitet beliebige GraphQL-Queries/Mutationen direkt an die GitHub GraphQL API weiter.

---

## 4. Sicherheits-Features & Hard Guardrails

- **Zero-Local-State**: Worker läuft vollständig serverless auf Cloudflare Edge ohne Dateisystem-Abhängigkeiten.
- **LLM-Platzhalter-Schutz**: Verhindert Code-Zerstörung! Payloads, die Text wie `// ... existing code ...`, `/* ... rest of code ... */` oder `# ... existing code ...` enthalten, werden vom Worker strikt mit einem Fehler (`400 Bad Request`) abgelehnt.
- **HEAD-OID-Prüfung**: Vor jedem Commit wird die aktuelle Commit-OID des Ziel-Branches abgefragt (`expectedHeadOid`). So werden Race-Conditions und unbemerkte Überschreibungen verhindert.
- **Tolerantes Patching**: Suchblöcke tolerieren Windows- (CRLF) und Unix-Zeilenumbrüche (LF) sowie leicht abweichende Einrückungen.

---

# Orchestrator Protocol, Modell-Disziplin & Logging-Verfassung

## 1. Modell-Politik: 3-Tier-Modellmatrix & Complexity-Gates

1. **Gesamtplanung & Strukturierung vorab mit Argon Medium (oder Argon High, `pro` / `inherit`):**
   - Sämtliche `/plan`-, Architektur-, Strukturierungs- und `#grillme`-Phasen für alle System-Parts laufen vorab auf **Argon Medium** (bzw. Argon High).
   - Jeder generierte Plan bewertet die Komplexität jedes Arbeitspakets auf einer Skala von `1–10` im `Execution Metadata`-Header.
2. **3-Tier Per-Batch Model Routing (Flash-First-Strategie):**
   - **Tier 1 (`flash_lite` — Score 1–3):** Trivial/mechanische Tasks (Kopieren, Löschen, Bereinigung, einfache Typen, Docs/Rules in Dateien < 300 Zeilen).
   - **Tier 2 (`flash` — Score 4–6, Default):** Standard-Implementierungen (<= 3–4 Dateien, klare Interfaces, Scoped Features, Unit-Tests in Dateien < 500 Zeilen) mit aktivem `/flash` Single-Action Protocol (keine `&&`/`;`-Verkettung).
   - **Tier 3 (`pro` / `inherit` — Score 7–10):** Hohe Komplexität, architekturkritische Eingriffe, Cross-Package-Refactorings oder Pro Decomposition.
3. **Multi-IDE & Live-Streaming Deep-Links:**
   - In Mode A (Google Antigravity) erzeugt der Orchestrator bei jedem Subagent-Start sofort einen klickbaren Stream-Link: `[⚡ Watch Stream](conversation://<conversation-id>)`.
   - Die Multi-IDE-Betriebsmodi (Mode A, Mode B, Mode C) sind verbindlich in [.agents/rules/multi-ide-setup.md](file:///y:/projekte2026/website/gitTrans/githubgeminibridge/.agents/rules/multi-ide-setup.md) definiert.

---

## 2. Minimal-Context Hub-and-Spoke & Durchgängiges Logging (Planung + Ausführung)

1. **Striktes Context-Window-Minimum im Hauptchat:**
   - Der Master-Orchestrator liest zur Orientierung ausschließlich `agents.md`, `plans/STATE.md`, die **letzten 25 Zeilen** von `plans/EXECUTION_LOG.md` sowie `plans/TEST_LOG.md`.
2. **Verpflichtendes Logging AUCH während Planung & Strukturierung (`EXECUTION_LOG.md` & `STATE.md`):**
   - Nicht nur Code-Ausführungen, sondern **auch jede Planungs- und Strukturierungsphase** muss in `plans/EXECUTION_LOG.md` und `plans/STATE.md` protokolliert werden:
     - Vor/bei einer Planungs- oder Strukturierungsphase: Eintrag `PLAN_START` / `PLAN_DONE` mit Zusammenfassung der Architektur-Entscheidung, dem ermittelten Complexity-Score (`X/10`), der Modell-Tier-Zuweisung (`FLASH_LITE`, `FLASH` oder `PRO`) und den vorbereiteten Blaupausen/Artefakten.
     - Bei jeder Code-Ausführung durch Subagenten: Eintrag `START` und `DONE` (oder `BLOCKED_NEEDS_USER_INPUT`) in `plans/EXECUTION_LOG.md` sowie strukturierter Prüfbericht in `plans/TEST_LOG.md`.
3. **Laufende Pflege von `agents.md`:**
   - Werden in einer Planungs- oder Strukturierungsphase neue Komponenten-Verträge, Routen oder `P1`-Prioritäten präzisiert, aktualisiert der Orchestrator `agents.md` (und die betroffene Datei in `.agents/rules/*.md`) direkt im selben Schritt.
4. **Crash- & Quota-Recovery:**
   - Bei einem Verbindungsabbruch oder `Quota Exceeded`-Event genügt in einem neuen Chat folgender Startbefehl:
     > *„Lies `agents.md`, `plans/STATE.md`, die letzten 25 Zeilen von `plans/EXECUTION_LOG.md` und `plans/TEST_LOG.md`. Setze die Orchestrierung am letzten Checkpoint gemäß unserer 3-Tier Modellpolitik fort.“*

---

## 3. Entscheidungs-Matrix des Auktionators (Modell-Routing)

| Task-Typ | Score & Tier | Ausführende Einheit | Begründung |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Architektur-Planung, `/plan`, `#grillme` & Strukturierung** | **Score 7–10 (`PRO`)** | Master-Orchestrator (`pro`/`inherit`) | Hohe Reasoning-Tiefe für Schnittstellen, `P1`-Invarianten und Impact-Mapping |
| **Komplexe Tasks & Multi-File-Refactorings** | **Score 7–10 (`PRO`)** | Subagent (`Model: 'pro'`) | Verhindert subtile Logik- oder Typbrüche über Paketgrenzen hinweg |
| **Standard-Implementierung & Scoped Features** | **Score 4–6 (`FLASH`)** | Subagent (`Model: 'flash'` + `#flash`) | Schnelle, kosteneffiziente Umsetzung anhand exakter Blaupausen & `shared/types.ts` |
| **Mechanische Cleanups, Typen & File-Ops** | **Score 1–3 (`FLASH_LITE`)** | Subagent (`Model: 'flash_lite'` / `'flash'`) | Minimale Latenz und Kosten für triviale Dateioperationen |
| **Parallele Paket-Ausführung (disjunkte Pfade)** | **Score <= 6 (`FLASH`)** | `#exec-wave` Subagenten (`flash`) | Disjunkte Datei-Inventare erlauben Scatter-Gather ohne Lock-Konflikte |
| **Vorab-Test, Smoke-Suite & Checkpoints** | **Score <= 3 (`FLASH_LITE`)** | Test-Subagent / Orchestrator | Deterministische Einzelbefehle (`tsc --noEmit`, `worker-smoke.mjs`, `git status`) |
| **Repo-Mix & Tarball-Streaming / AST-Syntaxcheck** | **0 LLM-Tokens (Edge/WASM)** | `mixer.ts` Stream / `oxc-parser` WASM | Reiner Binär-Stream bzw. Rust-WASM lokal im Cloudflare Worker (< 5 ms) |

---

## 4. Das `#solve` 4-Phasen Meta-Protokoll

1. **Phase 1 – Problem-Analyse & Impact-Mapping (Argon High):** Identifiziere alle direkt und indirekt betroffenen Dateien, `P1`-Prioritäten, Typ-Abhängigkeiten (`shared/types.ts`) und Edge-Cases.
2. **Phase 2 – Strukturierter Exekutions-Plan & Complexity-Check (Argon High):** Lege die Reihenfolge der Datei-Modifikationen und den Complexity-Score (`1–10`) fest; protokolliere `PLAN_DONE` in `plans/EXECUTION_LOG.md`.
3. **Phase 3 – Virtuelles Multi-Agenten-Review:**
   - *Architect:* Prüft Konformität gegen `agents.md` (`P1` aller Komponenten) und `.agents/rules/*.md`.
   - *Code Modifier:* Konstruiert minimale, eindeutige `search`/`replace`-Blöcke (ohne Platzhalter-Kommentare!).
   - *Reviewer:* Prüft auf vergessene Imports, Exporte und TypeScript-Typen.
4. **Phase 4 – Delegierte Umsetzung (Tier-Gated):** Im lokalen Projekt Ausführung durch isolierten Subagenten (Tier 1–3) inkl. `worker-smoke.mjs`-Verifikation; im Gemini-Webchat Ausgabe exakt **eines** fenced `json`-Codeblocks gemäß `GeminiPatchPayload`.

---
name: exec-wave
description: >-
  Orchestrates multi-batch roadmaps (such as 00_MASTER_ROADMAP.md) across serial and parallel waves using tiered subagents (flash_lite, flash, pro) and Multi-IDE fallbacks without bloating the main Coordinator context.
  Triggered by #exec-wave, /exec-wave, #wave-exec, or /wave-exec.
---

# Exec-Wave Workflow (`#exec-wave`)

**Purpose:** Execute multi-batch architectural plans (`plans/Batch_*.md`) organized into serial phases and parallel waves while keeping the main Coordinator agent's context window minimal and routing each batch to the most cost- and speed-effective model tier.

---

## Core Execution Protocol

1. **Read Only the Master Roadmap & Resolve Batch Tiers:**
   - Read the referenced roadmap file (e.g., `plans/00_MASTER_ROADMAP.md`) to identify the execution topology (Serial Phases vs. Parallel Waves) and each batch's complexity metadata (`[Score: X/10 | Tier: FLASH_LITE | FLASH | PRO]`).
   - **CRITICAL:** The Coordinator MUST NOT read the full target source files or implement code inline in the main conversation (unless operating in a Mode C Single-Agent IDE without subagents; see Section 5).
   - **3-Tier Per-Batch Model Selection (Flash-First Strategy):** Triggering `#exec-wave` / `/exec-wave` (or `#wave-exec` / `/wave-exec`) constitutes explicit user authorization to pass `Model: 'flash_lite' | 'flash' | 'pro'` to `invoke_subagent` (overriding default `'inherit'` in Mode A):
     - **Tier 1 (`flash_lite` — Score 1–3):** Trivial/mechanical batches (copy, simple types, static config, minor Tailwind/CSS in files <300 lines).
     - **Tier 2 (`flash` — Score 4–6, Default):** Standard scoped implementation batches (<= 3–4 files, clear interfaces, files <500 lines). If a batch has no explicit tier tag and touches only files <500 lines, default to `flash`.
     - **Tier 3 (`pro` — Score 7–10 or Files >=500 Lines):** Complex cross-file state refactors (Zustand, SSR/Hydration, Audio/Video lifecycles), files >=500 lines (`AGENTS.md` Mandatory Pro Decomposition), or high blast-radius architecture batches.

2. **Serial Phase Execution:**
   - For a serial batch (e.g., `plans/Batch_01_*.md`), spawn a single subagent via `invoke_subagent` using the batch's resolved tier:
     - **TypeName:** `self`
     - **Model:** `flash_lite` (Score 1–3) | `flash` (Score 4–6) | `pro` (Score 7–10)
     - **Role:** `Batch Executor`
     - **Prompt:** _"Read the execution plan at `<relative-path-to-batch.md>` and implement all file changes strictly as specified, adhering to `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` stability rules. Do NOT run git commit or shell commands; only perform file read/edit/write operations. Upon completing your assigned batch, report back strictly using this 3-point format:\n1. Status: [SUCCESS | BLOCKED | FAILED]\n2. Files Modified: [List relative paths modified/created]\n3. Summary: [1-2 sentences summarizing changes made or blockers encountered]\nDo NOT dump complete source files into the report."_
   - **Serial Launch Card (Clickable Stream Link):** Immediately after invoking `invoke_subagent`, output a launch card with a clickable deep link to the subagent's live session before entering the wait state:
     - Format: `[⚡ Watch Batch Live Stream](conversation://<subagent-conversation-id>)`
   - **Gather & Validation:** Wait for the subagent to report back, log its 3-point feedback summary, run the validation gate (`npm run lint` and `npm test`), and create the checkpoint commit on the active development branch (`preview` or dedicated `feature/*`/`task/*` branch; warn if on `main` or `master`).

3. **Parallel Wave Execution (Hybrid Scatter-Gather):**
   - For a parallel wave (e.g., Wave 1: `Batch_02`, `Batch_03`, `Batch_04`), verify from the roadmap that their File Inventories are strictly disjoint (zero overlapping files).
   - **Scatter:** Spawn one subagent per batch concurrently via `invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'self'`, `Role: 'Batch Executor'`, and its batch-specific `Model` (`flash_lite`, `flash`, or `pro`). Routine `flash` batches and complex `pro` batches can run side-by-side in the same wave so only the complex batch uses Pro tokens.
     - **Strict Subagent Rule:** Parallel subagents are strictly forbidden from running terminal/shell commands (`npm`, `tsc`, `git`) to prevent lock contention and race conditions. They must ONLY read, create, or edit their assigned files.
     - **Standard Subagent Prompt:** Every parallel subagent MUST be instructed with the standard 3-point feedback contract:
       _"Read the execution plan at `<relative-path-to-batch.md>` and implement all file changes strictly as specified, adhering to `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` stability rules. Do NOT run git commit or shell commands; only perform file read/edit/write operations. Upon completing your assigned batch, report back strictly using this 3-point format:\n1. Status: [SUCCESS | BLOCKED | FAILED]\n2. Files Modified: [List relative paths modified/created]\n3. Summary: [1-2 sentences summarizing changes made or blockers encountered]\nDo NOT dump complete source files into the report."_
   - **Wave Launch Dashboard (Mandatory Clickable Links):** Immediately after triggering `invoke_subagent`, the Coordinator MUST render a structured Wave Launch Dashboard table in chat before stopping tool calls to wait:
     ```markdown
     ### 🌊 Parallel Wave Launched: Wave X

     | Batch      | Tier & Model                | Assigned Scope / Files | Live Stream Link                                   |
     | :--------- | :-------------------------- | :--------------------- | :------------------------------------------------- |
     | `Batch_02` | `FLASH` (`flash`)           | `src/components/...`   | [⚡ Watch Batch_02 Stream](conversation://<id-02>) |
     | `Batch_03` | `FLASH_LITE` (`flash_lite`) | `src/types/...`        | [⚡ Watch Batch_03 Stream](conversation://<id-03>) |
     | `Batch_04` | `PRO` (`pro`)               | `src/store/...`        | [⚡ Watch Batch_04 Stream](conversation://<id-04>) |
     ```
   - **Gather & Auto-Escalation Gate:** Once all parallel subagents in the wave have reported back:
     1. **Wave Completion Summary:** Aggregate the subagents' 3-point reports into a concise bulleted summary in the main chat (Status, modified files, brief summary).
     2. **Sequential Validation:** Run `npm run lint` and `npm test` sequentially in the main Coordinator terminal.
     3. **Auto-Escalation (`FLASH` → `PRO`):** If a `flash_lite` or `flash` batch caused a type, lint, or test failure that survives **one** targeted Coordinator fix, immediately re-delegate that specific batch's files to a `pro` subagent (`TypeName: 'self'`, `Model: 'pro'`, `Role: 'Batch Executor'`). Output the escalation stream link: `[🚨 Watch Escalation Stream (PRO)](conversation://<pro-conversation-id>)`. Enforce the same 3-point feedback contract.
     4. **Red-Team Audit for Critical Batches:** If any batch in the wave had Score >= 8 (`Tier: PRO`), spawn a read-only Reviewer subagent via `invoke_subagent` with `TypeName: 'research'`, `Model: 'pro'`, `Role: 'Red-Team Auditor'` on that batch's modified files before committing. Output the reviewer stream link: `[🛡️ Watch Red-Team Audit](conversation://<reviewer-conversation-id>)`, and require a compact 2-point audit report (`1. Verdict: [PASS | BLOCKING_ISSUES]`, `2. Findings: [Concise file:line bullets or "None"]`).
     5. Stage all files from the wave explicitly by relative path (`git add <file1> <file2> ...`) and create a single consolidated checkpoint commit for the wave.

4. **Final Verification Gate (After Final Phase):**
   - Run the full repository verification suite sequentially (Single-Action Protocol):
     1. `npm run lint`
     2. `npm test`
     3. Run any domain-specific regression scripts required by the roadmap (e.g., `npx tsx scripts/media-canonicalization.regression.ts` when media or URL canonicalization logic was touched).
     4. `npm run check`
   - Bump the patch version in `package.json` once (`+0.0.1`, if `package.json` is not dirty with unrelated changes) and commit the final state on the active development branch (`preview` or dedicated `feature/*`/`task/*` branch; warn if on `main` or `master`, and never `git push` without explicit user instruction).

5. **Multi-IDE Capability Translation (Cross-IDE Portability):**
   - Adapt wave execution automatically to the host environment (see `.agents/rules/multi-ide-setup.md`):
     - **Mode A — Native Multi-Model Subagents (e.g., Antigravity):** Execute full parallel Scatter-Gather with `TypeName: 'self'` (or `'research'` for Red-Team audits) and per-batch `Model: 'flash_lite' | 'flash' | 'pro'`. Emit live clickable links via `[⚡ Watch Stream](conversation://<conversation-id>)` and aggregate the 3-point feedback reports in the Gather phase.
     - **Mode B — Fixed-Model or Rate-Limited Subagents (e.g., Kimi Code / CLI Agents):** Spawn subagents with `Model: 'inherit'` to preserve context isolation, enforce `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` and the 3-point feedback contract inside each subagent, and respect host concurrency rules (e.g., sequential subagent queue with pacing in `KIMI.md`). If the UI cannot handle `conversation://` URIs, output the subagent ID and the absolute path to its transcript file (`file:///<appDataDir>/brain/<conversation-id>/.system_generated/logs/transcript.jsonl`) as a readable fallback.
     - **Mode C — Single-Agent IDEs without Subagents (e.g., Cursor Composer / Cline):** Execute batches sequentially in strict context-hygiene passes (read only the current batch's target files, apply `.agents/skills/flash/SKILL.md` Single-Action Protocol, emit short 3-point feedback per batch pass, and validate per wave). Before starting a `Tier: PRO` batch (Score >= 7), emit a brief model-switch advisory if running on a fast/lightweight model, or perform an explicit self-review pass before staging.

---
name: solve
description: >-
  Investigates an issue, drafts an execution plan, and immediately delegates execution via the #delegate skill.
  Triggered by #solve or /solve.
---

# Solve Workflow (Investigate, Plan & Delegate)

- **Purpose:** Automatically investigate an issue, formulate a verified execution plan, and immediately delegate the entire execution pipeline to the `#delegate` skill (`delegate/SKILL.md`).

- **Step 1: Investigate and Plan**
  - Inspect the relevant repository context, diffs, and files to understand the problem.
  - Follow the global preflight, scope, and decision-gate contract in `AGENTS.md`; if material ambiguity exists, resolve it first using `#grillme`.
  - Formulate the concrete step-by-step implementation plan and affected file inventory.
  - Assess the **Task Complexity (1–10)**:
    - `<= 6`: Localized edits, unit tests, single component refactors, smoke checks.
    - `> 6`: Cross-package architecture, complex state flows, high blast-radius changes.

- **Step 2: Delegate via `#delegate` Workflow**
  - Hand over execution directly to the `#delegate` skill (`delegate/SKILL.md` or `.agents/skills/delegate.md`).
  - Follow all protocols defined in `delegate`:
    1. **Context Artifact:** Create the execution plan artifact via `write_to_file` (`RequestFeedback: false`, `ArtifactMetadata`).
    2. **Model Selection (3-Tier Intelligent Routing):**
       - Strict adherence to the `delegate` routing:
         - Explicit prompt overrides (`flash` or `pro`).
         - Complexity `<= 6` $\rightarrow$ `flash` model with Single-Action Protocol.
         - Complexity `> 6` $\rightarrow$ `pro` / `inherit`.
         - Fallback $\rightarrow$ `inherit`.
    3. **Subagent Invocation & Prompts:** Spawn `Artifact Executor` with the centralized prompt template including `.agents/rules/` synchronization.
    4. **Handoff & Monitoring:** Inform the user with both the plan link and the subagent session link (`[View Subagent Stream](conversation://<subagent-conversation-id>)`). Remind the user that the subagent progress can also be tracked live via the IDE's Subagent panel / background task indicator.
    5. Stop calling tools and wait for the subagent to report back.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Single Source of Truth:** Do NOT duplicate subagent invocation, prompt formatting, or model selection logic inside `solve`. Always follow and inherit the active `#delegate` skill specifications so future updates to `delegate` automatically take effect for `solve`.

File: packages/edge-worker/src/modules/linter.ts
================
import type { LinterError, GeminiPatchPayload } from '../types';
import {
  checkPlaceholderErrors,
  runBuiltinPreflightRules,
  type PreflightLinterHook
} from './patcher';
import {
  type OxcWasmParser,
  type OxcWasmParserFn,
  registerOxcWasmParser,
  validateSyntaxInWorker as scanSyntaxInWorker
} from './syntaxScanner';

export type { OxcWasmParser, OxcWasmParserFn };
export { registerOxcWasmParser };

/**
 * Structural syntax validation running in < 5 ms in Cloudflare Workers and Node.js.
 * Non-Fail-Fast: accumulates all detected syntax errors across the file.
 */
export async function validateSyntaxInWorker(filePath: string, content: string): Promise<LinterError[]> {
  return scanSyntaxInWorker(filePath, content);
}

/**
 * Runs all custom preflight rules without failing fast:
 * checks placeholder patterns (validateNoPlaceholders guardrail),
 * preflight.forbidPatterns, and preflight.requireExports.
 */
export function runCustomPreflightRules(
  files: { path: string; content: string }[],
  preflight?: GeminiPatchPayload['preflight']
): LinterError[] {
  const errors: LinterError[] = [];

  for (const file of files) {
    const phErrors = checkPlaceholderErrors(file.content, file.path);
    if (phErrors.length > 0) {
      errors.push(...phErrors);
    }
  }

  const builtinErrors = runBuiltinPreflightRules(files, preflight);
  if (builtinErrors.length > 0) {
    errors.push(...builtinErrors);
  }

  return errors;
}

/**
 * Full Stage 4 Pre-Flight Linter Hook implementation.
 * Iterates over all files to validate syntax and custom rules without failing fast,
 * deduplicating any identical error entries.
 */
export async function runPreflightLinter(
  files: { path: string; content: string }[],
  preflight?: GeminiPatchPayload['preflight']
): Promise<LinterError[]> {
  const errors: LinterError[] = [];

  // 1. Syntax check on all modified/created files
  for (const file of files) {
    const syntaxErrors = await validateSyntaxInWorker(file.path, file.content);
    if (syntaxErrors.length > 0) {
      errors.push(...syntaxErrors);
    }
  }

  // 2. Custom preflight rules (placeholders, forbidPatterns, requireExports)
  const customErrors = runCustomPreflightRules(files, preflight);
  if (customErrors.length > 0) {
    errors.push(...customErrors);
  }

  // Deduplicate errors by file:stage:line:col:message
  const seen = new Set<string>();
  const deduplicated: LinterError[] = [];

  for (const err of errors) {
    const key = `${err.file}:${err.stage}:${err.line ?? 0}:${err.column ?? 0}:${err.message}`;
    if (!seen.has(key)) {
      seen.add(key);
      deduplicated.push(err);
    }
  }

  return deduplicated;
}

File: packages/edge-worker/src/modules/patcher.ts

import type {
  FileChangeInput,
  PatchItem,
  FilePatchOperation,
  GeminiPatchPayload,
  WorkerPatchRequest,
  LinterError,
  WorkerPatchResponse
} from '../types';
import { utf8ToBase64 } from '../utils/encoding';
import {
  parseRepoOwner,
  getBranchHeadOid,
  createBranchRef,
  fetchMultipleFilesViaGraphQL,
  createCommitOnBranch
} from '../utils/github';
import { runPreflightLinter } from './linter';
import {
  type PreflightLinterHook as PreflightLinterHookType,
  checkPlaceholderErrors as checkPlaceholderErrorsImpl,
  runBuiltinPreflightRules as runBuiltinPreflightRulesImpl,
  applyPatchesInMemory as applyPatchesInMemoryImpl
} from './patchInMemory';

/**
 * Hard Guardrail: Patterns indicating AI placeholder text that would truncate code.
 */
export const LLM_PLACEHOLDER_PATTERNS = [
  /\/\/\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}/i,
  /#\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}/i,
  /\/\*\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}\s*\*\//i,
  /<!--\s*\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of|remaining|other)?\s*code\s*\.{0,3}\s*-->/i,
  /\.{3,}\s*(?:existing|rest of)\s*code\s*\.{3,}/i,
  /\/\/\s*\.{3,}\s*$/m,
];

/**
 * Stage 4 Linter Hook type: enables modules/linter.ts to inject AST/syntax checks
 * into the patch preflight pipeline without modifying patcher.ts.
 */
export type PreflightLinterHook = PreflightLinterHookType;

/**
 * Validates that file content does NOT contain LLM placeholder comments.
 * Throws an error immediately if detected (used by V1 Legacy endpoints).
 */
export function validateNoPlaceholders(content: string, filePath: string): void {
  for (const pattern of LLM_PLACEHOLDER_PATTERNS) {
    if (pattern.test(content)) {
      throw new Error(
        `Hard Guardrail Rejection: File "${filePath}" contains forbidden LLM placeholder text (e.g. "// ... existing code ..."). Commit rejected to prevent accidental code truncation.`
      );
    }
  }
}

/**
 * Non-throwing helper that checks LLM_PLACEHOLDER_PATTERNS and returns
 * aggregated LinterError items for Stage 2 & 4 non-fail-fast error reporting.
 */
export function checkPlaceholderErrors(content: string, filePath: string): LinterError[] {
  return checkPlaceholderErrorsImpl(content, filePath);
}

/**
 * Evaluates custom preflight rules without failing fast.
 * Verifies forbidPatterns and requireExports across all resulting files.
 */
export function runBuiltinPreflightRules(
  files: { path: string; content: string }[],
  preflight?: GeminiPatchPayload['preflight']
): LinterError[] {
  return runBuiltinPreflightRulesImpl(files, preflight);
}

/**
 * Extracts <<<<<<< SEARCH ... ======= ... >>>>>>> REPLACE blocks from text.
 */
export function parseSearchReplaceBlocks(text: string): PatchItem[] {
  const blocks: PatchItem[] = [];
  const regex = /<{5,9}\s*(?:SEARCH|ORIGINAL|HEAD)?\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n={5,9}\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n>{5,9}\s*(?:REPLACE|MODIFIED|UPDATED)?/g;
  let match: RegExpExecArray | null;

  while ((match = regex.exec(text)) !== null) {
    blocks.push({
      search: match[1],
      replace: match[2]
    });
  }

  return blocks;
}

/**
 * Applies a single search & replace transformation to a file content string.
 * Handles exact matches, CRLF vs LF differences, and trimmed line matches.
 */
export function applySearchReplace(original: string, search: string, replace: string, filePath: string = ''): string {
  // 1. Direct match
  if (original.includes(search)) {
    return original.replace(search, replace);
  }

  // 2. Normalized newline match (CRLF -> LF)
  const normOriginal = original.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
  const normSearch = search.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
  const normReplace = replace.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');

  if (normOriginal.includes(normSearch)) {
    const updated = normOriginal.replace(normSearch, normReplace);
    if (original.includes('\r\n')) {
      return updated.replace(/\n/g, '\r\n');
    }
    return updated;
  }

  // 3. Relaxed line-by-line trimmed match
  const origLines = normOriginal.split('\n');
  const searchLines = normSearch.split('\n');

  let matchStart = -1;
  for (let i = 0; i <= origLines.length - searchLines.length; i++) {
    let matches = true;
    for (let j = 0; j < searchLines.length; j++) {
      if (origLines[i + j].trim() !== searchLines[j].trim()) {
        matches = false;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (matches) {
      matchStart = i;
      break;
    }
  }

  if (matchStart !== -1) {
    const before = origLines.slice(0, matchStart).join('\n');
    const after = origLines.slice(matchStart + searchLines.length).join('\n');
    const updated = (before ? before + '\n' : '') + normReplace + (after ? '\n' + after : '');
    if (original.includes('\r\n')) {
      return updated.replace(/\n/g, '\r\n');
    }
    return updated;
  }

  const snippet = search.length > 200 ? `${search.slice(0, 200)}...` : search;
  throw new Error(`Search block could not be resolved for file "${filePath}". Target snippet:\n${snippet}`);
}

/**
 * Pure in-memory patch processor.
 * Applies patches, validates placeholders, checks idempotency and executes preflight rules.
 */
export async function applyPatchesInMemory(
  patches: FilePatchOperation[],
  existingFiles: Record<string, string | null>,
  preflight?: GeminiPatchPayload['preflight'],
  preflightHook: PreflightLinterHook = runPreflightLinter
): Promise<{
  additions: { path: string; content: string }[];
  deletions: { path: string }[];
  errors: LinterError[];
  hasEffectiveChanges: boolean;
}> {
  return applyPatchesInMemoryImpl(patches, existingFiles, preflight, preflightHook);
}

/**
 * Handles POST /api/patch requests (Stage 2 & Stage 4).
 * Performs concurrency checks, fetches files in 1 subrequest via GraphQL,
 * applies in-memory search/replace patches, runs preflight validations,
 * and commits atomically with createCommitOnBranch.
 */
export async function handlePatchRequest(
  req: WorkerPatchRequest,
  githubToken: string,
  preflightHook: PreflightLinterHook = runPreflightLinter
): Promise<WorkerPatchResponse> {
  if (!req.repo || !req.branch || !req.expectedHeadOid || !req.payload?.patches) {
    return {
      status: 'ERROR',
      errorMessage: 'Missing required fields: "repo", "branch", "expectedHeadOid", and "payload.patches" must be specified.'
    };
  }

  let owner = '';
  let repo = '';
  try {
    const parsed = parseRepoOwner(req.repo);
    owner = parsed.owner;
    repo = parsed.repo;
  } catch (err: any) {
    return {
      status: 'ERROR',
      errorMessage: err.message || 'Invalid repository format'
    };
  }

  const baseBranch = req.branch.trim();
  const effectiveTargetBranch = (req.targetBranch || req.branch).trim();
  const isNewTargetBranch = effectiveTargetBranch !== baseBranch;

  // 1. Concurrency Check: verify branch HEAD matches expectedHeadOid
  let currentHeadOid = '';
  let resolvedBranch = baseBranch;
  let repositoryId: string | undefined;
  try {
    const branchInfo = await getBranchHeadOid(owner, repo, baseBranch, githubToken);
    currentHeadOid = branchInfo.headOid;
    resolvedBranch = branchInfo.resolvedBranch;
    repositoryId = branchInfo.repositoryId;
  } catch (err: any) {
    return {
      status: 'ERROR',
      errorMessage: `Failed to resolve branch "${baseBranch}": ${err.message}`
    };
  }

  if (currentHeadOid !== req.expectedHeadOid) {
    return {
      status: 'GIT_CONFLICT',
      newHeadOid: currentHeadOid,
      errorMessage: `Expected HEAD "${req.expectedHeadOid}" but branch "${resolvedBranch}" is at "${currentHeadOid}". Fetch the latest repo-mix.xml and retry.`
    };
  }

  // 2. Batch-read all target files in 1 Subrequest via GraphQL
  const allTargetPaths = Array.from(new Set(req.payload.patches.map((p) => p.path)));
  let existingFiles: Record<string, string | null> = {};
  try {
    existingFiles = await fetchMultipleFilesViaGraphQL(owner, repo, resolvedBranch, allTargetPaths, githubToken);
  } catch (err: any) {
    return {
      status: 'ERROR',
      errorMessage: `Batch fetch failed for target files: ${err.message}`
    };
  }

  // 3. Apply Patches in Memory
  const patchResult = await applyPatchesInMemory(
    req.payload.patches,
    existingFiles,
    req.payload.preflight,
    preflightHook
  );

  // If preflight or search/replace errors occurred, collect and return all without committing
  if (patchResult.errors.length > 0) {
    return {
      status: 'PREFLIGHT_FAILED',
      errors: patchResult.errors
    };
  }

  // 4. Idempotency Check: if no effective changes, return NO_CHANGES_NEEDED
  if (!patchResult.hasEffectiveChanges) {
    return {
      status: 'NO_CHANGES_NEEDED',
      newHeadOid: currentHeadOid
    };
  }

  // 5. Target branch preparation (if targetBranch differs from baseBranch)
  let commitBranch = resolvedBranch;
  let commitExpectedHeadOid = req.expectedHeadOid;
  let createdBranch = false;

  if (isNewTargetBranch) {
    let targetExists = false;
    try {
      const targetInfo = await getBranchHeadOid(owner, repo, effectiveTargetBranch, githubToken);
      commitExpectedHeadOid = targetInfo.headOid;
      targetExists = true;
    } catch {
      targetExists = false;
    }

    if (!targetExists) {
      if (req.createBranchIfNeeded === false) {
        return {
          status: 'ERROR',
          errorMessage: `Target branch "${effectiveTargetBranch}" does not exist and createBranchIfNeeded is false.`
        };
      }
      try {
        await createBranchRef(owner, repo, effectiveTargetBranch, req.expectedHeadOid, githubToken, repositoryId);
        commitExpectedHeadOid = req.expectedHeadOid;
        createdBranch = true;
      } catch (err: any) {
        return {
          status: 'ERROR',
          errorMessage: `Failed to create target branch "${effectiveTargetBranch}": ${err.message}`
        };
      }
    }
    commitBranch = effectiveTargetBranch;
  }

  // 6. Encode additions to base64 for GraphQL createCommitOnBranch
  const encodedAdditions = patchResult.additions.map((a) => ({
    path: a.path,
    contents: utf8ToBase64(a.content)
  }));

  // 7. Execute atomic commit via createCommitOnBranch
  try {
    const commitResult = await createCommitOnBranch(
      owner,
      repo,
      commitBranch,
      commitExpectedHeadOid,
      req.payload.commitMessage || 'chore: apply patches via gemini-git-bridge',
      encodedAdditions,
      patchResult.deletions,
      githubToken
    );

    const compareUrl = `https://github.com/${owner}/${repo}/compare/${resolvedBranch}...${commitBranch}?expand=1`;

    return {
      status: 'COMMITTED',
      newHeadOid: commitResult.oid,
      commitUrl: commitResult.url,
      targetBranch: commitBranch,
      createdBranch,
      pullRequestUrl: isNewTargetBranch ? compareUrl : undefined
    };
  } catch (err: any) {
    const errMsg = err.message || '';
    if (
      errMsg.includes('expectedHeadOid') ||
      errMsg.includes('conflict') ||
      errMsg.includes('stale') ||
      errMsg.includes('HEAD')
    ) {
      const latestHead = await getBranchHeadOid(owner, repo, commitBranch, githubToken).catch(() => ({
        headOid: commitExpectedHeadOid
      }));
      return {
        status: 'GIT_CONFLICT',
        newHeadOid: latestHead.headOid,
        errorMessage: errMsg
      };
    }

    return {
      status: 'ERROR',
      errorMessage: `Commit execution failed: ${errMsg}`
    };
  }
}

/**
 * Resolves a FileChangeInput into full updated file content.
 * Kept for 100% backward compatibility with V1 routes (/push, /commit).
 */
export async function resolveFileContent(
  fileInput: FileChangeInput,
  existingContentFetcher: (path: string) => Promise<string>
): Promise<string> {
  const patchesToApply: PatchItem[] = [];

  // Check explicit single search/replace
  if (typeof fileInput.search === 'string' && typeof fileInput.replace === 'string') {
    patchesToApply.push({ search: fileInput.search, replace: fileInput.replace });
  }

  // Check array of patches
  if (Array.isArray(fileInput.patches)) {
    for (const p of fileInput.patches) {
      if (typeof p.search === 'string' && typeof p.replace === 'string') {
        patchesToApply.push(p);
      }
    }
  }

  // Check diff or content for <<<<<<< SEARCH blocks
  const diffToCheck = fileInput.diff || fileInput.content || '';
  if (diffToCheck.includes('<<<<<<<') || diffToCheck.includes('=====')) {
    const parsed = parseSearchReplaceBlocks(diffToCheck);
    patchesToApply.push(...parsed);
  }

  // If no search/replace patches found, treat as full file content replacement/creation
  if (patchesToApply.length === 0) {
    if (typeof fileInput.content === 'string') {
      validateNoPlaceholders(fileInput.content, fileInput.path);
      return fileInput.content;
    }
    throw new Error(`File change for "${fileInput.path}" must provide either "content" or search/replace patches.`);
  }

  // Validate that replace blocks do not contain placeholders
  for (const patch of patchesToApply) {
    validateNoPlaceholders(patch.replace, fileInput.path);
  }

  // Fetch existing content
  const existingContent = await existingContentFetcher(fileInput.path);
  let resolved = existingContent;

  for (const patch of patchesToApply) {
    resolved = applySearchReplace(resolved, patch.search, patch.replace, fileInput.path);
  }

  // Validate the final resolved file
  validateNoPlaceholders(resolved, fileInput.path);

  return resolved;
}

/**
 * Low-level TAR byte-stream parsing utilities.
 */

/**
 * Helper to concat two Uint8Arrays.
 */
export function concatBuffers(a: Uint8Array, b: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {
  const result = new Uint8Array(a.length + b.length);
  result.set(a, 0);
  result.set(b, a.length);
  return result;
}

/**
 * Parses a null-terminated ASCII string from a Uint8Array slice.
 */
export function parseAsciiString(buffer: Uint8Array, start: number, length: number): string {
  let end = start;
  const limit = start + length;
  while (end < limit && buffer[end] !== 0) {
    end++;
  }
  let str = '';
  for (let i = start; i < end; i++) {
    str += String.fromCharCode(buffer[i]);
  }
  return str.trim();
}

/**
 * Parses an octal integer from a TAR header field.
 */
export function parseOctal(buffer: Uint8Array, start: number, length: number): number {
  const raw = parseAsciiString(buffer, start, length);
  if (!raw) return 0;
  const parsed = parseInt(raw, 8);
  return Number.isNaN(parsed) ? 0 : parsed;
}

/**
 * Splits and parses lines from .repomixignore / .repomixignore.cf / .gitignore.
 * Strips comments starting with '#' and ignores whitespace-only lines.
 */
export function parseIgnoreLines(content: string): string[] {
  if (!content) return [];
  return content
    .split(/\r?\n/)
    .map((line) => line.trim())
    .filter((line) => line.length > 0 && !line.startsWith('#'));
}

/**
 * Checks whether a repository-relative path matches any ignore pattern.
 */
export function matchesIgnorePattern(relPath: string, patterns: string[]): boolean {
  if (!relPath || !patterns || patterns.length === 0) return false;
  const normalized = relPath.replace(/^\/+/, '');
  const segments = normalized.split('/');
  const fileName = segments[segments.length - 1];

  for (const rawPattern of patterns) {
    let pat = rawPattern.trim();
    if (!pat || pat.startsWith('#')) continue;

    const isDirOnly = pat.endsWith('/');
    if (isDirOnly) {
      pat = pat.slice(0, -1);
    }

    const isAnchored = pat.startsWith('/');
    if (isAnchored) {
      pat = pat.slice(1);
    }

    // Wildcard matching (*.csv, test_*, etc.)
    if (pat.includes('*') || pat.includes('?')) {
      const regexStr = pat
        .replace(/[.+^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')
        .replace(/\*/g, '.*')
        .replace(/\?/g, '.');
      const regex = new RegExp(isAnchored ? `^${regexStr}$` : `(^|/)${regexStr}$`);
      if (regex.test(normalized) || (!pat.includes('/') && regex.test(fileName))) {
        return true;
      }
      continue;
    }

    // Directory match
    if (isDirOnly) {
      if (isAnchored) {
        if (normalized === pat || normalized.startsWith(pat + '/')) return true;
      } else {
        if (segments.includes(pat) || normalized.startsWith(pat + '/') || normalized.includes('/' + pat + '/')) {
          return true;
        }
      }
      continue;
    }

    // Exact or prefix match
    if (isAnchored) {
      if (normalized === pat || normalized.startsWith(pat + '/')) return true;
    } else {
      if (fileName === pat || normalized === pat || normalized.startsWith(pat + '/') || segments.includes(pat)) {
        return true;
      }
    }
  }

  return false;
}

import { runLocalHybridTask, applyPatchesInMemory, extractJsonFromResponse } from './localRunner';
export { runLocalHybridTask, applyPatchesInMemory, extractJsonFromResponse };
import type { HybridTaskInput } from '../../../shared/types';

export function parseCliArgs(argv: string[]): {
  input: HybridTaskInput;
  model?: string;
  skipCommit?: boolean;
  help?: boolean;
} {
  const args = argv.slice(2);
  let prompt = '';
  let repo = 'local/repo';
  let branch = 'main';
  const targetPaths: string[] = [];
  let model: string | undefined;
  let skipCommit = false;
  let help = false;

  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
    const arg = args[i];
    if (arg === '--help' || arg === '-h') {
      help = true;
    } else if (arg === '--prompt' || arg === '-p') {
      prompt = args[++i] || '';
    } else if (arg === '--repo' || arg === '-r') {
      repo = args[++i] || 'local/repo';
    } else if (arg === '--branch' || arg === '-b') {
      branch = args[++i] || 'main';
    } else if (arg === '--path' || arg === '--target' || arg === '-t') {
      const val = args[++i];
      if (val) targetPaths.push(val);
    } else if (arg === '--model' || arg === '-m') {
      model = args[++i];
    } else if (arg === '--skip-commit') {
      skipCommit = true;
    } else if (!arg.startsWith('-') && !prompt) {
      prompt = arg;
    }
  }

  return {
    input: {
      prompt,
      repo,
      branch,
      targetPaths: targetPaths.length > 0 ? targetPaths : undefined
    },
    model,
    skipCommit,
    help
  };
}

export function printHelp(): void {
  console.log(`
Gemini Bridge Local CLI (Stage 5 Hybrid Routing)
Usage:
  gemini-local [options] [prompt]

Options:
  -p, --prompt <prompt>      Task instruction for Gemini
  -t, --path <filepath>      Target file(s) to include in context (repeatable)
  -r, --repo <owner/repo>    Repository name identifier (default: local/repo)
  -b, --branch <branch>      Branch name (default: main)
  -m, --model <model>        Gemini model (default: gemini-2.0-flash)
      --skip-commit          Apply changes to disk without creating a Git commit
  -h, --help                 Display this help message

Environment:
  GEMINI_API_KEY             Google Gemini API Key (or set in .env)
`);
}

export async function runCli(argv = process.argv): Promise<number> {
  const { input, model, skipCommit, help } = parseCliArgs(argv);

  if (help || !input.prompt) {
    printHelp();
    return help ? 0 : 1;
  }

  console.log(`[gemini-local] Starting local task: "${input.prompt}"`);
  if (input.targetPaths && input.targetPaths.length > 0) {
    console.log(`[gemini-local] Target files: ${input.targetPaths.join(', ')}`);
  }

  const result = await runLocalHybridTask(input, { model, skipCommit });

  if (result.status === 'SUCCESS') {
    console.log(`[gemini-local] Status: SUCCESS`);
    if (result.commitSha) {
      console.log(`[gemini-local] Commit SHA: ${result.commitSha}`);
    }
    if (result.message) {
      console.log(`[gemini-local] ${result.message}`);
    }
    return 0;
  } else if (result.status === 'PREFLIGHT_FAILED') {
    console.error(`[gemini-local] Preflight validation failed!`);
    if (result.errors) {
      for (const err of result.errors) {
        console.error(`  - [${err.stage}] ${err.file}:${err.line || 1} - ${err.message}`);
      }
    }
    return 2;
  } else {
    console.error(`[gemini-local] Error: ${result.message}`);
    return 1;
  }
}

import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import * as path from 'node:path';

// Auto-run only if directly executed as entry script
try {
  const currentFilePath = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
  const entryArg = process.argv[1] ? path.resolve(process.argv[1]) : '';
  const isDirectRun = entryArg === currentFilePath || (entryArg && entryArg.endsWith('gemini-local'));
  if (isDirectRun) {
    runCli().then((code) => {
      if (code !== 0) process.exit(code);
    });
  }
} catch {
  // If URL resolution fails, do not auto-run
}

import * as fs from 'node:fs';
import * as path from 'node:path';
import { execSync } from 'node:child_process';
import type {
  HybridTaskInput,
  HybridTaskResult,
  GeminiPatchPayload,
  FilePatchOperation
} from '../../../shared/types';
import { applyPatchesInMemory } from '../../edge-worker/src/modules/patchInMemory';
export { applyPatchesInMemory };

export interface LocalRunnerOptions {
  apiKey?: string;
  cwd?: string;
  model?: string;
  skipCommit?: boolean;
}

/**
 * Lightweight helper to load .env variables if not already set in process.env.
 */
export function loadEnvFile(cwd: string): void {
  const envPath = path.join(cwd, '.env');
  if (!fs.existsSync(envPath)) return;
  try {
    const raw = fs.readFileSync(envPath, 'utf-8');
    const lines = raw.split(/\r?\n/);
    for (const line of lines) {
      const trimmed = line.trim();
      if (!trimmed || trimmed.startsWith('#')) continue;
      const idx = trimmed.indexOf('=');
      if (idx > 0) {
        const key = trimmed.slice(0, idx).trim();
        let val = trimmed.slice(idx + 1).trim();
        if ((val.startsWith('"') && val.endsWith('"')) || (val.startsWith("'") && val.endsWith("'"))) {
          val = val.slice(1, -1);
        }
        if (!process.env[key]) {
          process.env[key] = val;
        }
      }
    }
  } catch {
    // Ignore .env read errors
  }
}

/**
 * Extracts JSON content from LLM markdown code blocks (```json ... ```).
 */
export function extractJsonFromResponse(raw: string): string {
  const trimmed = raw.trim();
  const jsonMatch = trimmed.match(/```(?:json)?\s*([\s\S]*?)\s*```/i);
  if (jsonMatch && jsonMatch[1]) {
    return jsonMatch[1].trim();
  }
  return trimmed;
}

/**
 * Calls Gemini REST API or SDK to generate GeminiPatchPayload.
 */
export async function callGeminiForPatch(
  prompt: string,
  contextFiles: { path: string; content: string }[],
  apiKey: string,
  model = 'gemini-2.0-flash'
): Promise<GeminiPatchPayload> {
  const fileContextString = contextFiles
    .map((f) => `### File: ${f.path}\n\`\`\`\n${f.content}\n\`\`\``)
    .join('\n\n');

  const systemInstruction = `You are a high-precision code modification agent.
You must respond ONLY with a valid JSON matching the GeminiPatchPayload schema.
Do NOT include explanations outside of the JSON block.
Schema:
{
  "commitMessage": "string",
  "preflight": {
    "forbidPatterns": ["optional regex string"],
    "requireExports": { "path/to/file.ts": ["exportName"] }
  },
  "patches": [
    {
      "path": "string",
      "action": "modify" | "create" | "delete",
      "blocks": [
        {
          "search": "exact string to replace",
          "replace": "new replacement string (never use placeholders like // ... existing code ...)"
        }
      ],
      "fullContent": "full content for create or full replacement"
    }
  ]
}`;

  const userContent = `Task Prompt:\n${prompt}\n\nContext Files:\n${fileContextString}`;

  // Try dynamic import of @google/genai first, fallback to REST API
  try {
    const genaiMod = await import('@google/genai' as any).catch(() => null);
    if (genaiMod && genaiMod.GoogleGenAI) {
      const ai = new genaiMod.GoogleGenAI({ apiKey });
      const response = await ai.models.generateContent({
        model,
        contents: `${systemInstruction}\n\n${userContent}`
      });
      const text = response.text || '';
      const jsonStr = extractJsonFromResponse(text);
      return JSON.parse(jsonStr) as GeminiPatchPayload;
    }
  } catch {
    // Fallback to REST API
  }

  // REST API Fallback
  const endpoint = `https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/${model}:generateContent?key=${apiKey}`;
  const res = await fetch(endpoint, {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      contents: [
        {
          role: 'user',
          parts: [{ text: `${systemInstruction}\n\n${userContent}` }]
        }
      ]
    })
  });

  if (!res.ok) {
    const errText = await res.text();
    throw new Error(`Gemini API error (${res.status}): ${errText}`);
  }

  const data: any = await res.json();
  const rawText = data?.candidates?.[0]?.content?.parts?.[0]?.text || '';
  if (!rawText) {
    throw new Error('Gemini API returned empty response');
  }

  const jsonText = extractJsonFromResponse(rawText);
  return JSON.parse(jsonText) as GeminiPatchPayload;
}

/**
 * Executes a local hybrid task:
 * 1. Reads target files from disk
 * 2. Asks Gemini for GeminiPatchPayload
 * 3. Applies patches in-memory and verifies with preflight linter
 * 4. Writes changes to disk and commits locally via Git
 */
export async function runLocalHybridTask(
  input: HybridTaskInput,
  options: LocalRunnerOptions = {}
): Promise<HybridTaskResult> {
  const cwd = options.cwd || process.cwd();
  loadEnvFile(cwd);

  const apiKey = options.apiKey || process.env.GEMINI_API_KEY;
  if (!apiKey) {
    return {
      mode: 'local',
      status: 'ERROR',
      message: 'GEMINI_API_KEY is not set in environment or options.'
    };
  }

  const targetPaths = input.targetPaths || [];
  const originalFiles: { path: string; content: string }[] = [];

  for (const relPath of targetPaths) {
    const absPath = path.resolve(cwd, relPath);
    if (fs.existsSync(absPath) && fs.statSync(absPath).isFile()) {
      originalFiles.push({
        path: relPath.replace(/\\/g, '/'),
        content: fs.readFileSync(absPath, 'utf-8')
      });
    }
  }

  try {
    // 1. Get patch proposal from Gemini
    const patchPayload = await callGeminiForPatch(
      input.prompt,
      originalFiles,
      apiKey,
      options.model
    );

    // 2. Validate in-memory with preflight linter (Stage 2 & 4)
    const existingRecord: Record<string, string | null> = {};
    for (const f of originalFiles) {
      existingRecord[f.path] = f.content;
    }
    for (const patch of patchPayload.patches) {
      if (!(patch.path in existingRecord)) {
        const absPath = path.resolve(cwd, patch.path);
        existingRecord[patch.path] = fs.existsSync(absPath)
          ? fs.readFileSync(absPath, 'utf-8')
          : null;
      }
    }

    const patchResult = await applyPatchesInMemory(
      patchPayload.patches,
      existingRecord,
      patchPayload.preflight
    );

    if (patchResult.errors.length > 0) {
      return {
        mode: 'local',
        status: 'PREFLIGHT_FAILED',
        errors: patchResult.errors,
        message: `Preflight checks failed with ${patchResult.errors.length} error(s).`
      };
    }

    // 3. Write changes to disk
    const modifiedPaths: string[] = [];
    for (const item of patchResult.deletions) {
      const absPath = path.resolve(cwd, item.path);
      if (fs.existsSync(absPath)) {
        fs.unlinkSync(absPath);
        modifiedPaths.push(item.path);
      }
    }

    for (const item of patchResult.additions) {
      const absPath = path.resolve(cwd, item.path);
      fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(absPath), { recursive: true });
      fs.writeFileSync(absPath, item.content, 'utf-8');
      modifiedPaths.push(item.path);
    }

    // 4. Git commit
    let commitSha: string | undefined;
    if (!options.skipCommit && modifiedPaths.length > 0) {
      try {
        const commitMsg = patchPayload.commitMessage || `Gemini Bridge local patch for: ${input.prompt.slice(0, 50)}`;
        for (const p of modifiedPaths) {
          execSync(`git add "${p}"`, { cwd, stdio: 'pipe' });
        }
        execSync(`git commit -m "${commitMsg.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`, { cwd, stdio: 'pipe' });
        commitSha = execSync('git rev-parse HEAD', { cwd, stdio: 'pipe' }).toString().trim();
      } catch (gitErr: any) {
        return {
          mode: 'local',
          status: 'SUCCESS',
          message: `Files written to disk, but git commit failed: ${gitErr.message}`
        };
      }
    }

    return {
      mode: 'local',
      status: 'SUCCESS',
      commitSha,
      message: `Successfully applied local patch (${modifiedPaths.length} file(s) modified).`
    };
  } catch (err: any) {
    return {
      mode: 'local',
      status: 'ERROR',
      message: err.message || 'Unknown error occurred during local execution',
      errors: [err]
    };
  }
}
